No new REST resource — two new optional fields on the existing `POST /v1/containers` (`restart`, default `"no"`; `depends_on`, an ordering-only list). `restart: "always"` persists the exact create request (`daemon/src/containerdef.c`/`daemon/include/containerdef.h`, new) alongside creating it live immediately, replayed verbatim at every future daemon boot and after any unprompted exit. See ADR-0025 for the full account, including a critical, confirmed-before-design constraint (`confirm_boot()`'s own "healthy means about to serve traffic, nothing more" definition, which auto-start must never disturb) and two real bugs found and fixed during verification: a double-pidfd-registration bug (crashed the daemon outright on the first real restart-across-reboot test) and a `DELETE /v1/containers/{name}` gap that made a definition permanently stuck once it failed to autostart even once (a `depends_on` cycle or unknown dependency) — surfaced by cross-test state leakage in the full regression sweep, fixed by decoupling "stop the live instance" from "remove the persisted definition."

`handle_create()` was split into a thin HTTP wrapper and a reusable `create_container_from_body()` (zero HTTP coupling), shared by the live REST path, boot-time `containerdef_autostart_all()` (runs once, right after `confirm_boot()`, unconditionally, blocking like every other startup step already is), and crash-restart replay. Crash restart is the reactor's first-ever timer: a one-shot `timerfd_create()`-based delay (2s, fixed, daemon-wide) rather than a blocking `sleep()`, which would freeze the entire single-threaded, non-blocking event loop for the whole delay. `depends_on` resolution mirrors `pkg.c`'s own `resolve_chain()` shape exactly (DFS + a `visiting[]` cycle-detection stack), applied to container names instead of package names. `DELETE /v1/containers/{name}` now also removes the persisted definition, permanently, in the same call — confirmed directly with the user as the intended v1 model (no separate "pause vs. disable").

Verified end-to-end against a live daemon, not just at the unit level: a fast-exiting `restart: "always"` container was observed crash-looping with a real, measured ~2.0s gap between each exit and the next (different) pid appearing; a daemon restart brought a persisted container back with a fresh pid; `DELETE` was confirmed to both stop it and leave it gone after a subsequent restart. The entire pre-existing REST-facing test suite was re-run unchanged after the `handle_create()` refactor, confirming zero regressions, before any new test was added.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen (closed by Part 3 below):** no per-service restart delay or backoff policy (a single fixed daemon-wide constant for v1); no `restart: "on-failure"`/`"unless-stopped"` variants (Docker's fuller policy set) — only `"always"`/`"no"`, extendable later if actually needed.

### Phase 13 part 2 (done): TCP readiness checks for `depends_on`

Part 1 left `depends_on` as start-order only — a dependency being "started" (its `clone3()` returned) said nothing about whether it was actually ready to serve, a real gap for the user's own stated case (a router depending on DNS actually answering, not just having a pid). New optional `"readiness": {"tcp_port": N, "timeout_seconds": N}` (default 30, bounded 1–300) on the same `POST /v1/containers` request `restart`/`depends_on` already extended, following the exact same shape (out-parameters through `create_container_from_body()`, cached fields on `struct container_def`, echoed live in `GET /v1/containers` from `containerdef_find()`) — see ADR-0026 for the full design reasoning, including why TCP-only, boot-time-only, and best-effort were each the right v1 boundary.

`wait_for_tcp_ready()` (`daemon/src/main.c`) is a plain, blocking, retried `connect()` against the container's own primary network address (`entry->nets[0].ip_be`) — no non-blocking-connect-plus-`poll()` needed, since the destination is always a directly L2-adjacent bridge network the daemon itself manages (a refused/not-yet-listening port returns `ECONNREFUSED` immediately, never hangs). Called only from `containerdef_autostart_all()`, right after each definition's own successful autostart and before moving on to whatever depends on it — never from the live `POST` path (a container never waits on its own readiness at creation) and never from crash-restart (which runs inside the reactor's non-blocking event loop, where any blocking wait would violate the design ADR-0025 already established; crash-restart's own dependents are normally already running/ready anyway, since only the one container crashed). A readiness check that never succeeds logs a warning and lets boot proceed regardless — best-effort, matching the existing "one bad thing shouldn't take down the rest of boot" posture a `depends_on` cycle or unknown dependency already has.

Verified end-to-end against a live, restarted daemon (`test/test_container_restart.c`, extended, plus a new `test/tcp_listen_child.c` fixture that only starts listening after a deliberate 2s delay): a dependent's autostart was confirmed to genuinely wait — the whole daemon-restart-to-healthy window measured at least 1s, dominated by the dependency's own 2s readiness delay, not just present-in-code; a readiness check that never succeeds (1s timeout, nothing ever listening) still let its dependent autostart afterward, proving best-effort holds and boot never hangs; `readiness` without any network attachment was confirmed rejected with 400 at creation time. 3 consecutive clean runs; full regression sweep (`test_daemon`, `test_cli`, `test_networks`, `test_dns`, `test_pki`, `test_daemon_net`, `test_daemon_devices`, `test_pkg`, `test_images`) re-run clean against the rebuilt daemon.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** no exec-inside-namespace or HTTP readiness probe (TCP-only for v1); readiness isn't consulted on crash-restart (a stated, deliberate boundary, not an oversight — see ADR-0026); no per-dependency override of the daemon's blocking-wait behavior (always synchronous at boot, for every definition that configures it).

### Phase 13 part 3 (done): restart policy expansion + backoff

Part 1 deliberately shipped only `restart: "always"`/`"no"` and a single fixed 2s crash-restart delay, deferring both Docker-style restart policy variants and any per-service/backoff delay. Both closed here, together. See ADR-0027 for the full design reasoning.

`restart` gains `"on-failure"` (restarts only after a nonzero-exit/signal-killed crash, not a clean `0` exit -- but this decision only ever governs the crash-restart timer, never boot-time autostart, since `exit_status` is never persisted and so can't survive a daemon restart -- a stated, permanent v1 boundary) and `"unless-stopped"` (behaves like `"always"`, except a new `POST /v1/containers/{name}/stop` -- reusing `registry_remove()`'s own existing SIGKILL+reap verbatim, no new kill primitive -- is remembered across a daemon restart; `"always"`/`"on-failure"` get a fresh chance at every boot regardless of a prior stop, matching Docker's own real `--restart=always` behavior). Since "a persisted definition exists at all" no longer implies `"always"` on its own, `struct container_def` gains an explicit, persisted `restart_policy` field -- a `container_defs.json` written by parts 1/2 has no such key, so its absence on load defaults to `"always"`, matching the old implicit semantics exactly (`No Regressions`). "Start" is still just a re-`POST /v1/containers` with the same body, same as it's always been -- `containerdef_add()` now also explicitly clears the new `stopped` flag on every call.

New optional `restart_delay_seconds` (1-300, default 2, the old fixed constant) overrides only the *base* crash-restart delay; automatic backoff on top of it (doubling per consecutive failure, capped at 30s, reset once a container's own uptime reaches 30s before it exits again) is uniform and not itself a further per-container knob. A non-persisted `consecutive_failures` counter on `struct container_def` naturally resets to 0 every daemon boot. A real, non-obvious consequence confirmed while writing this part's own test suite: a container's first-ever failure already applies one doubling (the failure counter goes 0 -> 1 on that very first fast exit), so a 5s base's real first restart delay is ~10s, not 5s -- the first draft of the test itself got this wrong (too-tight polling windows around the wrong expected value), not the daemon; fixed by correcting the test's own expectations after confirming the daemon's actual behavior directly with a temporary debug trace.

Verified end-to-end against a live, restarted daemon: `on-failure` confirmed to skip a restart after a clean exit but perform one (with a real, measured, backed-off delay) after a crash; `restart_delay_seconds` confirmed honored; a `stop` issued mid-delay-window confirmed to permanently cancel that pending restart (closing a real race `handle_restart_timer_event()` would otherwise have -- the registry entry is already gone and a timer already armed by the time a stop can race it); backoff confirmed genuinely growing across two consecutive fast-crash cycles, contrasted against a second, always-stable container landing at the un-doubled base delay instead (proving the reset branch, since a single container can't be made to alternate fast-crash vs. stable-run between restarts -- the daemon replays its exact same command verbatim every time, by design); `unless-stopped` confirmed to stay down across a real daemon restart while an identically-stopped `always` container came back. 3 consecutive clean runs; full pre-existing regression suite (all 17 binaries) re-run clean.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** no `SIGTERM`-then-grace-period-then-`SIGKILL` for `POST .../stop` -- reuses DELETE's own instant-SIGKILL semantics exactly, a deliberate boundary; no exit-history persistence, so `on-failure`'s clean-exit memory never survives a daemon restart (see ADR-0027); backoff's cap/stability-reset thresholds are fixed daemon-wide constants, not configurable.

## Phase 14: GPU passthrough

ADR-0017 (Phase 12 part 1) explicitly named GPU passthrough as the next intended consumer of its own PCI/USB device-passthrough mechanism. The user's own stated end goal includes GPU passthrough to an LLM container, on AMD hardware specifically.

### Phase 14 part 1 (done): GPU discovery + grouped device grants

A real GPU needs several `/dev` nodes granted together (for AMD: `/dev/dri/cardN` + `/dev/dri/renderDN`), not the single node every other bus grants today. See ADR-0028 for the full design reasoning.

New `"gpu"` bus in `daemon/src/device.c` (`enumerate_gpu()`), mirroring `usb`/`pci`/`net`'s existing shape: walks `/sys/class/drm/*`, groups multiple DRM nodes (`cardN`, `renderDN`) belonging to the same physical GPU under one stable `gpu:<idx>` (resolved via each node's own `device` symlink back to its parent PCI address), emitting one `discovered_device` per real node (`gpu:<idx>:<node>`) -- exactly the same "one id, one `/dev` node" shape every other bus already has. `enumerate_pci_one()`'s existing placeholder-suppression (already skips re-listing a network controller generically since `net:` represents it) gained PCI class `03` (display controller) alongside the existing `02`, so a GPU surfaces once, under `gpu:`, not twice.

The new part is the *grant* side: a new, additive `device_find_group()` (`device_find()` itself untouched) resolves a bare `gpu:<idx>` logical id -- never itself a listed `GET /v1/devices` entry -- into every one of that GPU's currently assignable member nodes at once, so `--device=gpu:0` grants everything a GPU needs in one atomic request instead of an operator naming each node by hand (a real, confirmed-with-the-user preference over the one-id-per-node alternative). `create_container_from_body()`'s device-grant loop (`daemon/src/main.c`) changed from a strict 1:1 request-index-to-grant-slot mapping to a decoupled read/write-index loop, since one requested id can now expand into several grants -- bounds-checked against `CONTAINER_MAX_DEVICES` incrementally as groups expand, not against the raw request array's own length. `GET /v1/containers` reflects the real expanded member grants, not an echo of what was requested.

Verified over real HTTP against a live daemon, to the extent actually possible in this sandbox (no GPU driver bound to anything here -- confirmed directly, `/sys/class/drm` exists but is empty): `GET /v1/devices` returns cleanly with zero `gpu:` entries; `POST /v1/containers` with `"devices":["gpu:0"]` correctly 400s via `device_find_group()`'s genuine empty-expansion path, a real exercise of the new code; every pre-existing `usb:`/`pci:`/`net:` grant scenario re-verified unchanged. 3 consecutive clean runs; full pre-existing regression suite re-run clean.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen (explicit part 2):** kernel driver enablement (`CONFIG_DRM`/`CONFIG_DRM_AMDGPU`, currently absent from `image/kernel/qemu-part1.config`) and firmware-blob staging (`amdgpu` needs `linux-firmware` files present under `/lib/firmware/amdgpu/` on the host root at driver-probe time -- a new concern for `image/src/mkbootroot.c`, which has never staged firmware) -- real, separate work, deliberately not bundled into part 1. The positive "grouped id expands into real, working grants" path is unverifiable in this sandbox regardless of design (no bound GPU driver anywhere here) -- the same documented boundary ADR-0022's real NIC passthrough already has, deferred to real hardware.

### Phase 14 part 2 (done): GPU kernel driver, firmware staging, KFD discovery

Part 1 built discovery and grouped grants but the kernel had no GPU driver at all -- `amdgpu` wasn't built in, so nothing part 1 built could actually do anything on real hardware yet. Two more real gaps closed alongside it: firmware (the driver loads blobs via `request_firmware()` at boot-time probe, before any container exists, so they have to live on the host root) and `/dev/kfd` discovery (ROCm compute's own device node, entirely separate from the `/sys/class/drm` nodes part 1 already found -- without it, a `gpu:0` grant couldn't actually run compute work, only display). See ADR-0029.

`image/kernel/qemu-part1.config` gained `CONFIG_FW_LOADER`/`CONFIG_DRM`/`CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER`/`CONFIG_DRM_AMDGPU`/`CONFIG_HSA_AMD` -- verified the way this file's own history requires (two real past incidents, `docs/roadmap/ROADMAP.md`'s own Phase 11 part 1 section, where `scripts/config --enable` alone silently dropped options its own Kconfig dependency graph didn't actually satisfy): a real kernel source fetch, `make allnoconfig`, `merge_config.sh` with the updated fragment, `make olddefconfig`, then a programmatic check that *every* `CONFIG_X=y` in the entire fragment -- not just the 5 new lines -- landed as `=y` in the resulting `.config`, not silently reverted. A full `make bzImage` was then run to prove the config actually compiles, not just configures -- and the resulting kernel was booted for real: `test_boot`/`test_boot_ab`/`test_installer` all re-run clean against it.

Firmware isn't vendored into this repo -- confirmed directly with the user, fetched fresh from upstream `linux-firmware` at build time instead (this dev sandbox itself has zero amdgpu firmware anywhere, and the distro package isn't even available here, so there was no "copy from the build host" shortcut available the way `ld.so`/`libc.so.6`/`libtinfo.so.6` already get). `image/src/mkbootroot.c` gained a new required 5th argv, `firmware_dir` (empty string means skip -- all 3 existing test call sites pass it explicitly, keeping the test suite's fast/no-network posture completely unaffected), staged via the existing `copy_dir_files()` reused verbatim, not reimplemented -- relying on `linux-firmware`'s own `amdgpu/` directory being flat, confirmed directly against the real upstream repo (675 files, zero nested), not just assumed. New `test/test_mkbootroot_firmware.c` proves the staging mechanism itself against a synthetic scratch directory. `daemon/src/device.c`'s `enumerate_gpu()` (part 1) gained a pass over `/sys/class/kfd/kfd` -- one shared, system-wide node, not per-card -- emitting a `gpu:<idx>:kfd` member for every discovered GPU group.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** genuine end-to-end proof (a real bound AMD GPU, ROCm actually seeing `/dev/kfd`, a container computing on it) is unverifiable in this sandbox regardless of design -- no GPU driver is bound to anything here, confirmed directly -- the same documented boundary ADR-0022/ADR-0028 already have, deferred to real hardware; KFD's own host-wide (not per-GPU) exposure on a multi-GPU host is a real, permanent ROCm/KFD architectural property, not a gap a future Kanxeo change can close at the grant level (see ADR-0029).

## Phase 15 (done): per-container config files + generalized sysctls

Raised directly by the user: several containers built from the *same* image (a shared `bird` binary) each needing their own `bird.conf` and their own bash orchestration script, plus sysctls beyond just `ip_forward` (`rp_filter`, relevant to policy-based routing) -- and, before this, there was no operator-facing way to put a file into a container at all. See ADR-0030.

New `"files"` field on `POST /v1/containers` is a purely daemon-side, pre-`clone3()` mechanism -- the daemon already `mkdir()`s a container's own overlay `upperdir` on its own host process before `registry_create()`/`clone3()` runs, so files are written straight into it via plain `open()`/`write()` before the container's own process ever exists, meaning a staged file (or script, referenced directly by `cmd`) is already there the instant it `execve()`s -- no `struct container_spec`/runtime-library change needed at all. Path validation (leading `/`, no `.`/`..` components) is the one place in this feature with real security weight, since a validated path becomes a real host filesystem write target -- a fresh, purpose-built validator, since no existing path/name validator in the codebase was reusable as-is. New `"sysctls"` field extends (not replaces) the existing `ip_forward` mechanism (`container_net_enable_ip_forward()`, `src/container_net.c`) with a general `container_net_apply_sysctl()`, deliberately restricted to keys starting with `net.` -- the one sysctl subtree the kernel actually namespaces end to end; most others (`vm.*`, `fs.*`) are host-wide regardless of netns, so allowing arbitrary keys would be a real host-wide write primitive from inside a container.

Verified against a live daemon: a real file lands at the right host path with the right content and mode; `../../etc/passwd` and a no-leading-`/` path both `400`; a `net.ipv4.conf.all.rp_filter` sysctl confirmed via `nsenter` into the container's own `/proc/<pid>/ns/net` to land inside *that* netns specifically while the host's own value stayed untouched -- real per-netns isolation, not just a successful write syscall; a non-`net.*` key and a malformed key (`net..foo`) both `400`. Because `restart:"always"` already persists the whole request body verbatim and replays it on every boot/crash-restart (ADR-0025), `files`/`sysctls` are automatically re-staged/re-applied correctly on every replay for free.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** no volume/bind-mount concept for anything beyond small, content-inlined files -- a large binary asset or a directory tree still has no home in this model, not needed by the use case that motivated this part; `registry_create()`'s own parameter list keeps growing (now 11 parameters) -- a real, deferred cleanup, not attempted here.

## Phase 16: host OS update mechanism + automatic package updates

### Phase 16 part 1 (done): host + package update mechanism

The A/B rollback machinery (ADR-0014, Phase 11 part 2) had existed since the very first install, but no operator-facing tool could ever *write* a new OS image into the inactive slot on an already-running system -- root B stayed genuinely empty forever, by design, until this phase. Raised directly by the user, alongside the parallel package-update gap (`pkg install --upgrade` already existed, but nothing triggered it automatically). See ADR-0031 for the full design reasoning covering both halves.

New `POST /system/update` (`do_system_update()`/`handle_system_update()`, `daemon/src/main.c`) writes a fresh control-plane squashfs onto whichever root slot isn't this boot's own (`g_slot`, promoted from a plain `main()` local to a file-scope static so the new handler can reach it, same precedent as `g_epfd`), then stages a fresh systemd-boot loader entry for it with a fresh Automatic Boot Assessment tries-left counter -- the exact shape `kanxeo-install.c`'s own `populate_esp()` already uses for the very first install, now reachable on a live system. `image_path` is a local path the operator has already `scp`'d onto the box (no upload/streaming HTTP machinery -- `HTTP_MAX_REQUEST_SIZE` is far too small for a real image, and the charter has no registry to plug into anyway); a cheap squashfs-magic check (`"hsqs"`) runs before anything is written, turning a wrong path into a clean `400` rather than corrupting the inactive slot. New fixed `ROOT_A_DEVICE`/`ROOT_B_DEVICE` macros follow the exact same "known QEMU virtio-blk layout, GPT-name resolution can wait for real hardware" precedent `ESP_DEVICE`/`CONFIG_DEVICE`/`CONTAINERS_DEVICE` already established, rather than reintroducing `kanxeo-install.c`'s installer-only `find_partition_device()` into the daemon. Deliberately does **not** reboot -- the operator calls the pre-existing `POST /system/reboot` separately once ready to cut over; `confirm_boot()`/the boot counter, both entirely unchanged, decide whether the fresh slot sticks.

New `POST /pkg/update-all` (`handle_pkg_update_all()`) is the "automagic" package half: a new `pkg_find_update_candidate()` (`daemon/src/pkg.c`) walks every installed package and reuses the exact same fresh-recipe-parse-and-compare `write_pkg_json()` already performs per-entry for `GET /pkg`'s own `available_version` field, returning the first one that's drifted -- then hands it straight to the pre-existing `pkg_install_start(..., upgrade=1, ...)`, no second rebuild path. Starts at most one upgrade, honestly respecting the existing v1 single-install-in-flight constraint rather than working around it; an operator or cron entry drains a whole backlog by calling it again once each job finishes.

Verified two different ways for the two different risk profiles: `POST /system/update`'s pure validation logic (no `--slot=`, missing/unreadable `image_path`, bad squashfs magic) needs no real device at all -- `boot_init()` (the only thing that would need one) only runs under `--init-mode`, so a plain dev daemon with `--slot=a` already exercises every check past the slot check itself (`test/test_system_update.c`, 3 consecutive clean runs). The real device-write/loader-entry-write path genuinely needs a real virtio-blk disk, so it's proven inside a real QEMU guest (`test/test_boot_update.c`): a `--test-update-image=` self-test flag on `kanxeod` (same precedent as `--simulate-unhealthy-boot`) calls `do_system_update()` directly against a real, distinguishable second squashfs staged on a raw scratch partition, since host-to-guest HTTP is unavailable for a statically-addressed guest under this harness (see this file's own SLIRP note); the freshly-written slot's on-disk bytes are confirmed byte-exact against the source image, its loader entry confirmed present on the ESP via `mtools`, and a second, independent QEMU power-on against the same disk confirmed to actually boot the freshly-updated slot -- the real payoff, not just that bytes landed somewhere. `pkg update-all` verified against a real installed package with a bumped `pkg_version=` recipe: starts exactly one real upgrade job, and reports nothing to do once the backlog is drained (`test/test_pkg.c`, extended). Full clean rebuild, zero warnings; full pre-existing regression suite re-run clean.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen (closed by part 2 below):** `POST /system/update` covers the root only -- the kernel (`kanxeo-bzImage` on the ESP) is shared by both A/B slots and entirely untouched by this mechanism, a real gap ADR-0014 itself had already named as a future target ("kernel updates, kanxeod binary updates") but this part didn't close.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen (still open):** no scheduled/periodic trigger for either endpoint (both are on-demand, operator- or cron-invoked, matching what MISSION.md's "automagic" language actually calls for -- a real scheduling subsystem is a separate, deliberately deferred concern); no automatic "update then reboot" chaining -- a real deployment's own maintenance-window policy, not this daemon, should own that timing decision; the daemon still has no general GPT-partition-name resolution of its own (see ADR-0031's Consequences).

### Phase 16 part 2 (done): per-slot kernel updates

Part 1 left the kernel out: both A/B loader entries hardcoded the identical `linux /kanxeo-bzImage`, one shared file with no way to update it without corrupting whichever slot was currently booted. Raised directly by the user immediately after part 1 shipped, once they understood `/system/update` didn't cover the kernel. See ADR-0032.

The kernel becomes per-slot, the same way the root already is: `kanxeo-bzImage-a`/`kanxeo-bzImage-b` on the ESP, each loader entry referencing only its own. `kanxeo-install.c`'s `populate_esp()` now pre-stages *both* slots' kernel files identically at install time (unlike root B's own deliberate emptiness -- a kernel is small enough that duplicating it removes a real edge case, an operator's first root-only update to slot B failing for a reason unrelated to what they actually asked to change, for near-zero cost). `POST /system/update`'s `image_path` and `kernel_path` are now independent, both-optional request fields (at least one required) -- update just the root, just the kernel, or both together. `kernel_path` gets the same validation posture `image_path` already has: a real magic check (the x86 boot-sector signature at offset `0x1FE` plus `struct setup_header`'s own `"HdrS"` magic at `0x202`, confirmed directly against a real `build/bzImage`) before anything is written, both fields fully validated before either is written so one bad field never leaves the other half-applied. `write_file_to_device()` was split into a shared `copy_bytes()` core plus two thin, flag-specific callers (the existing raw-device one, and a new `write_file_to_esp()` for the kernel file -- the ESP is a real, already-mounted filesystem, not a raw partition, and needs `O_CREAT` where a device doesn't). Response gains `"updated"` (`["root"]`/`["kernel"]`/`["root","kernel"]`).

Verified inside a real QEMU guest (`test/test_boot_update.c`, extended): a combined root+kernel self-update call reports both updated; the resulting `kanxeo-bzImage-b` is confirmed byte-exact against the source kernel; the fresh loader entry's own content, read back directly, is confirmed to reference `/kanxeo-bzImage-b` specifically; `kanxeo-bzImage-a` is confirmed byte-unchanged throughout; a second, independent QEMU boot actually boots successfully from `kanxeo-bzImage-b` -- the real proof the per-slot resolution works, not just that bytes landed somewhere. Deliberately reuses the same real kernel bytes as both the initial slot-A kernel and the "new" `kernel_path` source (the same precedent `test_boot_ab.c` already established for reusing identical squashfs content across slots to prove the *selection mechanism*) rather than requiring a second, genuinely different, real bootable kernel build for what the test is actually trying to prove. Fast validation (`test/test_system_update.c`, extended): `kernel_path` missing/nonexistent/bad-magic, and a valid `image_path` combined with a bad `kernel_path`, all `400` against a plain dev daemon, no QEMU needed. 3 consecutive clean runs on every new/changed test; full pre-existing regression suite re-run clean, including the QEMU-based `test_boot`/`test_boot_ab`/`test_installer` (all touched by or adjacent to the new per-slot ESP layout).

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** no scheduled/periodic trigger (same open item part 1 already has, now covering the kernel too); the daemon still has no general GPT-partition-name resolution of its own.

## Phase 17 (done): platform state backup/restore

Kanxeo had no backup, export, or restore mechanism at all. Raised directly by the user while preparing a real, one-shot, no-rollback migration of a production home lab from Proxmox -- for an irreversible cutover, no backup story is a structural risk, not a missing nicety. Confirmed with the user: this had to follow the same API-first methodology every other subsystem already does (a container, `kanxeoctl`, or a host cron job triggers it as a pure REST client; the daemon owns the only real mechanism). See ADR-0033.

Scope, deliberately bounded and confirmed with the user before any code was written: platform *configuration* state only -- container definitions, networks, DNS records, package install state and recipes. **Not** workload data (each container's own persistent data is its own concern, with its own native export tooling -- Kanxeo has no volume-sharing or exec-into-container capability to reach into it anyway). **Not** image rootfs content (reproducible by re-running `pkg install` against what `pkg_installed` already records, since everything is compiled from source -- this captures the "shopping list," not the built bytes). **Never** PKI -- the CA and every issued leaf certificate's own private key has never been returned over the API by existing, deliberate design, and this feature does not carve out an exception; PKI backup stays a separate, host-level concern (`/var/lib/kanxeo/pki/`, backed up directly, outside the API).

New `GET /system/backup` (`do_system_backup()`/`handle_system_backup()`, `daemon/src/main.c`) bundles each state file's raw content as an escaped JSON string (reusing `ConfigFile.content`'s own existing shape, not a second raw-embedding convention), read via the existing `persist_read_file()`. New `POST /system/restore` (`do_system_restore()`/`handle_system_restore()`) is the reverse: independent, all-optional fields (at least one required), every field validated as real JSON (or, for `pkg_recipes`, an object of strings) *before* any file is written via the existing `persist_atomic_write()`, mirroring `do_system_update()`'s "validate everything, then write" discipline. Restore deliberately does not reboot or hot-reload -- restored files only take effect on the *next* boot, through `containerdef_autostart_all()` and its siblings, entirely unchanged; this needed no new daemon-initialization code at all, only "the files are now correct." `kanxeoctl backup [--output=PATH]`/`restore --input=PATH` round-trip the bundle byte-for-byte, not re-serialized.

Verified end-to-end against a live, twice-restarted daemon (`test/test_system_backup.c`, no QEMU needed -- pure file I/O + JSON): a real persisted container, network, and DNS record created; `GET /system/backup` confirmed to embed matching content; the container deleted entirely (live instance and persisted definition both gone); `POST /system/restore` with just the earlier-captured `container_defs` field; a real daemon restart afterward confirmed the deleted container came back -- proof the write plus the *existing*, untouched boot-time replay path genuinely reconstructs state. A malformed field confirmed rejected `400` with the real on-disk file provably byte-for-byte untouched; restore confirmed to not hot-reload. 3 consecutive clean runs; full pre-existing regression suite re-run clean.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** no scheduled/periodic backup trigger (same on-demand, operator-/cron-invoked posture `/system/update`/`/pkg/update-all` already have); no automated image-content or workload-data restore (both real, separate, out-of-band steps by design, not silently deferred); PKI backup has no tooling of its own yet, by design -- it stays a manual, host-level, outside-the-API concern.

## Phase 18 (done): kernel video console + interactive kanxeoctl shell

Raised directly by the user after their first real Proxmox install: the installed system had no video console at all -- only serial ever showed anything, on both Proxmox's own console viewer and real hardware -- and every `kanxeoctl` invocation required re-typing `--host=...` from scratch, with no way to just stay on the box and issue commands one after another.

`image/kernel/qemu-part1.config` had zero console/framebuffer support (`CONFIG_VT`, `CONFIG_FRAMEBUFFER_CONSOLE`, any `CONFIG_FB_*`/`CONFIG_DRM_*` -- none present), even though the loader entry already passed `console=tty0 console=ttyS0` (`daemon/src/main.c`, `image/src/kanxeo-install.c`, `image/src/mkinstalleriso.c`) -- the kernel was told to use a video console that had nothing behind it. Fixed with the modern, driver-agnostic path: `CONFIG_VT`/`CONFIG_VT_CONSOLE`/`CONFIG_FRAMEBUFFER_CONSOLE` (the console-subsystem side) plus `CONFIG_SYSFB_SIMPLEFB`/`CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM`/`CONFIG_DRM_FBDEV_EMULATION` (rides the EFI GOP framebuffer UEFI firmware already sets up, via DRM's `simpledrm` driver -- works without any GPU-specific driver bound to anything, unlike the older `CONFIG_FB_EFI` fbdev-only path). Verified the way this file's own history requires (real kernel source fetch, `make allnoconfig`, `merge_config.sh`, `make olddefconfig`, then a programmatic check that every fragment symbol -- not just the new ones -- landed as `=y`), then a real `make bzImage`. This sandbox's own QEMU harness captures serial output only, so there's no way to automate "a human looking at a video console sees text" from inside it -- the achievable proxy: the kernel's own boot-time `printk()` messages reach every registered console including serial, and the captured log confirmed `simpledrm` bound (`[drm] Initialized simpledrm 1.0.0 for simple-framebuffer.0`, `Console: switching to colour frame buffer device 160x50`, `fb0: simpledrmdrmfb frame buffer device`) on both boot stages of a real `test_boot` run. Final confirmation that Proxmox's own console viewer or a real monitor shows text is necessarily the user's own check, the same boundary GPU passthrough and Secure Boot already have.

`cli/src/main.c` gained an interactive shell: bare `kanxeoctl` (with or without `--host=`/`--port=`/`--json`, no command) now checks `isatty(STDIN_FILENO)` -- on a real terminal it enters `run_shell()`, a plain `fgets()` loop with a small quote-aware (not full shell-grammar) tokenizer, dispatching each typed line through a `dispatch_command()` extracted verbatim from `main()`'s existing one-shot chain (one dispatch table, not two copies drifting apart). Piped/non-tty invocations with no command keep the exact prior "print usage, exit 2" behavior, so scripts never hang waiting on a prompt they can't see. `exit`/`quit`/EOF end the loop; `help` reuses `print_usage()`; a failed command prints its existing error and continues the session rather than exiting the process. Deliberately no GNU readline (no history/arrow-key editing) -- `kanxeoctl` links against nothing but this project's own code today, and readline would be its first external runtime dependency, not warranted for what was asked ("keep it simple"). `--host=`/`--port=`/`--json` stay session-wide, set once at startup -- a deliberate v1 boundary, not an oversight.

One real bug found and fixed during interactive testing (a genuine `pty`-based session, not just the non-tty gating check): `tokenize_line()` only split on space/tab, so `fgets()`'s trailing `\n` stayed attached to the last token -- `"exit\n"` never matched `"exit"`, so even `exit` fell through to "unknown command" and the shell could never be left from the keyboard. Fixed by also treating `\n`/`\r` as token delimiters.

Verified: `make allnoconfig`/`merge_config.sh`/`olddefconfig` confirmed every fragment symbol landed as `=y`; a full `make bzImage` succeeded; `test_boot`/`test_boot_ab`/`test_installer`/`test_boot_update` each re-run 3 consecutive times against the new kernel with identical passing results, including the framebuffer-console-init confirmation above. A real `pty`-based interactive session against a live daemon confirmed `health`, an unknown command (error printed, session continues), `ps`, and `exit` (clean loop exit, process exit 0) all behave as designed; non-tty gating re-confirmed unchanged. Full clean rebuild, zero warnings; full pre-existing regression suite (all 20 non-QEMU binaries plus the 4 QEMU-based ones) re-run clean.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** the much larger web dashboard redesign the user asked about in the same conversation (tree-based Proxmox-style navigation, full API parity across images/devices/system actions/richer container fields, architected so the dashboard can later be split out to run as its own separate container) -- deliberately deferred to its own future planning cycle, not bundled into this phase.

## Phase 19 (done): real console login -- keyboard input + PID1-spawned kanxeoctl shell

Phase 18 shipped a video console that displays text and gave `kanxeoctl` an interactive REPL, but booting the resulting ISO on real Proxmox hardware surfaced two real gaps neither piece actually closed: the video console had no keyboard input driver at all (Phase 18 only added the framebuffer *output* path), and nothing on the installed system ever launched `kanxeoctl` on any console -- `kanxeod` runs as PID 1 with no getty, no login, no shell of any kind, and `kanxeoctl` itself wasn't even staged into the installed root image. Raised directly by the user immediately after using the Phase 18 ISO for real, the same "shipped, then a real gap surfaced once actually used" pattern Phase 16 part 1 -> part 2 already established. See ADR-0034.

`image/kernel/qemu-part1.config` gained PS/2 (`CONFIG_KEYBOARD_ATKBD`, which itself selects `SERIO`/`SERIO_LIBPS2`/`SERIO_I8042`) and USB HID (`CONFIG_USB`/`CONFIG_USB_XHCI_HCD`/`CONFIG_HID`/`CONFIG_USB_HID`) keyboard input drivers -- covers both Proxmox's typical emulated PS/2 keyboard today and real USB keyboards on actual hardware for the eventual bare-metal migration. Two more menuconfig gates (`CONFIG_INPUT_KEYBOARD`, `CONFIG_USB_SUPPORT`, `CONFIG_HID_SUPPORT`) had to be added explicitly, caught by exactly the verification discipline this file's own history requires: a first build attempt without them left `USB`/`USB_PCI`/`USB_XHCI_HCD`/`USB_XHCI_PCI`/`HID`/`HID_GENERIC`/`USB_HID` all silently unset despite being listed in the fragment, caught by the required post-merge `=y` check rather than reaching a real boot with no keyboard support.

`image/src/mkbootroot.c` now stages `kanxeoctl` into the installed root at `/bin/kanxeoctl` (a new required argv, `kanxeoctl_bin`, via a second `test_image_fixture_build()` call) -- every call site (`test_boot.c`, `test_boot_ab.c`, `test_boot_update.c` x2, `test_installer.c`, `test_mkbootroot_firmware.c`, `README.md`'s build recipe) updated in the same pass, the precedent already set when `firmware_dir` was added as a required 5th arg.

`daemon/src/main.c` gained a PID1-supervised console shell: two new reactor patterns reusing existing shapes rather than inventing new ones -- `spawn_console_shell()`/`register_console_shell_pidfd()`/`handle_console_shell_event()` mirror `register_pkg_fetch_pidfd()`/`handle_pkg_fetch_event()`'s pidfd+epoll reaping exactly; `arm_console_respawn_timer()`/`handle_console_respawn_timer_event()` mirror `arm_restart_timer()`/`handle_restart_timer_event()`'s timerfd-based delay exactly. Once boot is healthy (`if (init_mode)`, right after `containerdef_autostart_all()`), `kanxeod` forks a child per console (`/dev/tty0`, `/dev/ttyS0`), each calling `setsid()` then opening its own tty (making it that session's controlling terminal via standard Linux tty semantics -- no explicit `TIOCSCTTY` needed, and what keeps a console Ctrl-C from ever reaching `kanxeod` itself) before `execve()`ing `/bin/kanxeoctl --host=<g_bind_addr> --port=<g_port>` with no command, landing straight in `run_shell()` (Phase 18). Exiting (or a crash) triggers a fixed 2-second respawn, forever -- the same "always there" posture a real getty already has, with the delay guarding against a tight fork loop if the binary or tty is ever genuinely broken. No authentication gate: this daemon has zero authentication anywhere today, so a login-free console introduces no new privilege boundary (see ADR-0034).

Verified two ways, matched to what's actually testable in this sandbox: the kernel's keyboard driver stacks confirmed binding in a real captured boot log (`input: AT Translated Set 2 keyboard`, `usbcore: registered new interface driver usbhid`); and a new `test/test_console_shell.c` scripts a genuine end-to-end round-trip over the serial console via `qemu_boot_capture()`'s existing `scripted_input` mechanism -- exit the first spawned shell instance, wait for the *respawned* instance's own genuinely new prompt (real proof the pidfd-reap + timerfd-respawn path fired, not just that the initial spawn worked), then confirm that respawned instance answers a real `health` call. 3 consecutive clean runs on the new test; `test_boot`/`test_boot_ab`/`test_installer`/`test_boot_update` each re-run 3 consecutive times against the new kernel + kanxeoctl-staged image with identical passing results; full clean rebuild, zero warnings; full pre-existing regression suite (all 20 non-QEMU binaries) re-run clean.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** actual keyboard-driven interaction on the video console -- a human physically typing at Proxmox's console viewer and seeing it register -- is not automatable in this sandbox (`qemu_boot_capture()` runs with `-monitor none`/`-display none`, no QMP channel to synthesize a keypress even in principle) and remains the user's own final check, the same boundary GPU passthrough, Secure Boot, and Phase 18's own framebuffer output already have; `kanxeod`'s own stdout/stderr still share `ttyS0` (the console `console=` argument ordering makes it `/dev/console`) with that console's own shell, an accepted, documented cosmetic limitation, not solved here (see ADR-0034); the much larger web dashboard redesign remains deferred to its own future planning cycle, unaffected by this phase.

## Phase 20 (done): fix silent real-install breakage in PKI/pkg, portable build-toolchain artifact

While answering how to get real, non-C, network-fetched software (gitea) onto a Kanxeo host, the user proposed building packages inside a real, durable build-toolchain container rather than depending on whatever host `kanxeod` happens to run on. Checking that proposal surfaced two real, live-confirmed bugs that had nothing to do with the proposal itself: `pki ca bootstrap` failed outright (`CA genpkey failed`, HTTP 500) on a genuinely fresh installed image -- booted one and ran it on the real console to confirm, not guessed at. See ADR-0035.

**Root cause 1**: `kanxeod` shells out to `/usr/bin/openssl`/`/usr/bin/curl`/`/usr/bin/tar`/`/usr/bin/sha256sum`/`/usr/bin/cp`/`/bin/rm` (grep-confirmed against `daemon/src/pki.c`'s/`daemon/src/pkg.c`'s own hardcoded `_BIN` macros -- the authoritative list, not `docs/roadmap/ROADMAP.md`'s own partly-stale prose, which also names `dnsmasq`, something `kanxeod` itself never shells out to), but `mkbootroot.c` never staged any of them onto the installed root -- only `kanxeod`/`kanxeoctl` and their own bare runtime libs. Hidden this whole time because every test in this project's history runs `kanxeod` on this rich dev sandbox, never the genuinely minimal squashfs root real hardware boots. Fixed: `mkbootroot.c` now stages all six, each with its own real shared-library closure (captured via `ldd` on this build host), the same precedent `test_dns.c`'s own `DNSMASQ_LIBS[]` already established for a real third-party binary with a real dependency closure. One dependency ran a level deeper than expected -- `openssl req -x509` also needed its own default config file (`/usr/lib/ssl/openssl.cnf`, a symlink into `/etc/ssl/openssl.cnf` on this build host) -- found by a second live failure after the binary itself was staged, not guessed at up front.

**Root cause 2, more fundamental**: `pkg_seed_image_runtime()` -- the function seeding `ld.so`/`libc.so.6`/`libtinfo.so.6` into *every* newly created image (ADR-0023) -- read from `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/...`, but `mkbootroot.c` only ever writes those files to `/lib64/...`/`/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/...` (no `/usr` prefix) on the installed root. This silently broke *every* `image create` on a real install, not just PKI/pkg -- confirmed by direct path comparison against `mkbootroot.c`'s own literal write paths (this dev sandbox's own merged-`/usr` symlinks made both path forms resolve identically here, masking the bug locally). Fixed by correcting the three source paths.

**The user's actual ask**: `pkg_bootstrap_build_image()`'s own toolchain staging has the identical live-host-copy problem -- correct on this rich dev sandbox, silently empty on a real install. `test_image_fixture_stage_toolchain()` (extracted into `test/test_image_fixture.c`, already the shared location both tests and `image/src/` build tools reuse) is now the one place "what a toolchain needs" is defined -- shared by `pkg_bootstrap_build_image()` (kept, now a thin wrapper, the dev/test-convenience fallback) and new `image/src/mktoolchainimage.c` (a standalone build-time tool, mirroring `mkbootroot.c`'s own shape, producing one real, portable squashfs artifact -- built manually, occasionally, the same posture `build/bzImage` itself already has). Extended the existing extras list with `/usr/local/go` (this sandbox's own real Go install, tolerant-if-absent) -- a low-cost step toward Go-buildable packages like gitea, though no gitea recipe itself is proven by this phase (Go module fetching under this system's network-less build constraint, any frontend-asset build, CGO/`sqlite` considerations -- real, separate follow-up work). New `pkg_bootstrap_from_toolchain()` imports the artifact via a real `unsquashfs -f -no-xattrs -d` (userspace extraction, no mount/loop-device -- this sandbox's own documented "no `/dev/loop*` at all" constraint). `POST /v1/pkg/bootstrap` gains an optional `toolchain_path` field -- a local path the operator has already `scp`'d onto the box, the same "local path, not an upload" precedent `/system/update`'s `image_path`/`kernel_path` already established; omitting it keeps today's exact bare-`POST` contract. `kanxeoctl pkg bootstrap` gains a matching `--toolchain=PATH`.

Verified two ways, one for each real bug: `test/test_console_pki_bootstrap.c` (new) boots a genuinely fresh install and scripts `pki ca bootstrap` over the console -- must now succeed (before this phase, confirmed, it didn't), the real regression guard against root cause 1 recurring. `test/test_console_pkg_bootstrap.c` (new) proves the toolchain-artifact mechanism itself end-to-end: builds a real ~607MB toolchain squashfs on this sandbox (`mktoolchainimage`), writes it to a scratch partition, boots fresh with a new `--test-bootstrap-toolchain=` self-test flag (same precedent `--test-update-image=` already established for exercising a device-write code path no HTTP call can reach in this harness), and confirms a real `gcc` binary landed afterward -- not just that the call returned OK. Found and fixed two real problems building this specific test: the decompressed toolchain content (~2.0GB, `du`-confirmed, not the ~607MB compressed size assumed at first) exceeded what the default 512MB-RAM guest's tmpfs-backed `/var/lib/kanxeo` fallback could hold (a throwaway-test-topology limit, not a real one -- a real install has a real ext4 containers partition, ADR-0018), fixed by adding a `mem_mib` field to the shared `qemu_boot_opts`; and `unsquashfs` itself exited nonzero on a benign xattr-unsupported-by-tmpfs warning, fixed with `-no-xattrs` (the tool's own diagnostic named the fix). `pkg_seed_image_runtime()`'s fix relies on `test_pkg.c`'s own pre-existing per-image seeding coverage (step 13, unchanged) rather than a new dev-sandbox test -- this sandbox's merged-`/usr` layout makes the old buggy path and the new correct path resolve to the same files, so no test running here, old code or new, can actually discriminate between them; the QEMU-based tests above are what would have caught it. 3 consecutive clean runs on both new tests; full clean rebuild, zero warnings; full pre-existing regression suite re-run clean.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** a real, working gitea recipe -- this phase proves the toolchain-import *mechanism* with a known-good C recipe path, not gitea itself; the much larger web dashboard redesign remains deferred, unaffected by this phase.

## Phase 21 (done): git/gitea recipes, multi-source package recipes, and a real LDAPS deployment

Three real, individually-verified recipes proving out different corners of the source-based package system, plus the platform's first genuinely TLS-secured workload service. Raised directly by the user across a sequence of related asks: a git server, then an LDAP server "as light as DNS, all containers should be able to use it," then wiring that LDAP server up with LDAPS using Kanxeo's own CA.

`pkg/recipes/git.recipe` and `pkg/recipes/gitea.recipe`: real, from-source builds (git via its own `make`/`configure`, gitea via a real offline `make backend` using the Go toolchain staged in Phase 20), each individually proven through the actual `kanxeod` pipeline, not just locally.

**Multi-source package recipes (ADR-0036).** Writing a real `lldap.recipe` -- chosen over `glauth` specifically for its genuine, first-party web UI, confirmed directly against both projects' own READMEs after the user's stated hard requirement ruled out `glauth` (no web UI at all) -- surfaced a real structural limit: lldap's web frontend compiles via Rust->WASM, but its own real build (per its `Dockerfile`) also fetches 8 small external CDN files (Bootstrap, Bootstrap Icons, Font Awesome, 3 fonts) that the network-less `pkg_build()` container can't reach, and the recipe format supported only one `pkg_source=`/`pkg_sha256=` pair. `pkg_source=`/`pkg_sha256=` become space-separated, positionally-paired lists (`PKG_MAX_SOURCES=16`); index 0 is extracted as before (zero behavior change for every pre-existing recipe); indices 1+ are copied verbatim into `/build/extra/<basename>`; fetched via nested `fork()`/`execve()`/`waitpid()` inside the one already-tracked pidfd child, never a shell script, so a recipe-supplied URL is never shell-interpolated. All-or-nothing verification across every source, matching the existing single-source guarantee. `pkg/recipes/lldap.recipe` doubly verified: a real local build first (every CDN asset's sha256 confirmed, and independently cross-checked against the SRI sha384 hashes lldap's own `index_local.html` hardcodes), then a full real install through `kanxeod` itself.

**Wiring lldap up with real LDAPS surfaced four more real, live-confirmed bugs, none of them hypothetical:**

- `wasm-pack` (lldap's WASM build tool) failed outright inside the isolated `pkg_build()` container with "couldn't find your home directory, is $HOME not set?" -- the container's fixed environment (`PKG_DESTDIR`, `PATH`) never included `HOME`. Fixed: `g_build_envp` (`daemon/src/pkg.c`) now also sets `HOME=/build`, a real writable directory every build container already has.
- Fixing that surfaced a second, deeper one: `wasm-pack` also needs to install its own `wasm-bindgen-cli` (a version pinned by lldap's own `Cargo.lock`) via `cargo install`, which the network-less build container can't do (`Could not resolve host: index.crates.io`). Fixed the same way `wasm-pack`/the `wasm32-unknown-unknown` target themselves already are: pre-populated once on the real build host via wasm-pack's own `WASM_PACK_CACHE` env var (confirmed real via wasm-pack's own `src/cache.rs`), landing under `/usr/local/cargo/wasm-pack-cache/` -- rides along for free with the existing wholesale `/usr/local/cargo` toolchain staging, no new staging entry needed. `lldap.recipe`'s `pkg_build()` now exports `WASM_PACK_CACHE` to match.
- The real lldap binary, once it actually ran, failed with `error while loading shared libraries: libgcc_s.so.1: cannot open shared object file` -- `pkg_seed_image_runtime()`'s `runtime_libs[]` (ADR-0023) only ever seeded `ld.so`/`libc.so.6`/`libtinfo.so.6` into a freshly built image, never the unwinding/math libs a Rust binary also links. Fixed by extending that same list with `libgcc_s.so.1` and `libm.so.6`, staged the identical no-`/usr`-prefix way the existing three already are -- a systemic fix, not an lldap-specific one, since any future Rust or C++ package will need both.
- A real, load-bearing race in `--pki-issue` itself, not just the theoretical one already named when this session started investigating it: `pki_cert_create()` correctly refuses to recreate a cert that already exists (`PKI_ERR_DUPLICATE`), but `daemon/src/main.c`'s `pki_issue` block treated *any* non-`PKI_OK` result, including `DUPLICATE`, as a hard failure that skipped `pki_cert_deliver()` entirely. That's fine on a container's very first start, but fatal on every restart after -- confirmed live: lldap's own process starts fast enough to try reading its TLS cert before delivery finishes on the first attempt (a real instance of the `register_container_pidfd()`-before-`pki_issue` ordering already named as a risk before this phase), and every `restart:"always"` respawn after that first failure hit `DUPLICATE` and *never* reached delivery again -- a permanent crash loop, not a transient one. Fixed: `DUPLICATE` is now treated as "the cert already exists, deliver it" rather than a failure, restoring the actual intent of `restart:"always"` as a race mitigation. Verified against a real crash (`kill -9` the running lldap process) and a real daemon restart (replaying the persisted `restart:"always"` definition) -- both come back up with LDAPS bound correctly on the very next attempt.

**The LDAPS deployment itself** needed no other new mechanism -- pure composition of what already existed: a local TOML config (`assets_path`, `database_url`, the top-level `key_file`, and `ldaps_options.cert_file`/`key_file` all set to absolute paths under `/opt/lldap`) staged via the pre-existing `--file=` mechanism (ADR-0030, written into the container's upperdir before `clone3()`, so it's present from the very first line of `main()`); `--pki-issue --pki-cert-dir=/opt/lldap` issuing and delivering a real cert from Kanxeo's own root CA, CN/SAN matching the container's own name; `--restart=always` as the crash-restart mitigation for the startup-ordering race, now actually effective per the fix above. No CWD/`--workdir=` gap needed solving at all (a real risk this phase started out expecting to hit) -- setting every lldap config path absolute sidesteps `run`'s complete lack of a working-directory option entirely, cleaner than the shell-wrapper-plus-staged-`bash` approach originally anticipated.

Verified end-to-end against a live daemon: `openssl s_client -connect <ip>:6360 -CAfile ca.crt -verify_hostname ldapsvc` returns `Verify return code: 0 (ok)`, chain resolving genuinely back to Kanxeo's own root CA; `ldapsearch -H ldaps://ldapsvc:6360 -x -D "uid=admin,..." -b "dc=kanxeo,dc=local"` performs a real bind and returns real directory entries over that same TLS connection. Full clean rebuild, zero warnings; full pre-existing non-QEMU regression suite (20 binaries) re-run clean, including every test that autostarts the real persisted `ldapsvc` container against shared daemon state.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** no DNS integration for the LDAP service yet (`--dns-register` alongside `--pki-issue` would let `ldaps://ldapsvc` resolve for real clients instead of needing a manual hosts entry, as used for this phase's own verification) -- a real, small follow-up, not attempted here since it wasn't asked for; LDAPS is proven for this one container, not yet adopted as a platform-wide "every container authenticates against it" convention the user asked about thinking through earlier in this arc -- that remains a deliberate, separate future decision.

## Phase 22 (done): network gateway becomes optional, VLAN + physical-NIC bridge attachment

Raised directly by the user, from a real topology they wanted to build: two core router containers on a shared L2 segment, each with their own IP, sharing a VRRP address as the segment's actual gateway, running a routing protocol between them -- with a plain workload container attaching to that same segment pointed at the VRRP address, not at anything the host owns. That topology was impossible: `network create` always created a bridge *and* always assigned host-part `.1` as a real IP address on the bridge device itself, permanently reserved, with every attached container unconditionally getting a default route installed toward it -- the host was forced to be the L3 gateway for every network it hosted, no opt-out. The same conversation surfaced two adjacent real gaps: no VLAN support anywhere, and no way to enslave a real physical host NIC to a Kanxeo-managed bridge (only whole-NIC passthrough into one container's own netns existed, ADR-0022). See ADR-0037 and ADR-0038.

Three explicit decisions confirmed with the user before any code was written: (1) flip the default outright -- new networks are gateway-less unless a gateway is explicitly requested, not an opt-in flag alongside the old behavior; (2) build VLAN support in the same phase; (3) build physical-NIC-to-bridge enslavement in the same phase.

**Gateway optionality (ADR-0037).** `network create` gains an optional `gateway` field/`--gateway=A.B.C.D`. Omitted (the new default): the bridge is created with no host-owned IP at all -- pure L2. Given, the address is validated (real, in-subnet, not network/broadcast) and assigned to the bridge, the same "gateway lives on the bridge, never a port" convention ADR-0011 already established, just operator-chosen now instead of hardcoded to `base|1`. `network_spec`/`container_net_child_configure()` (`src/container_net.c`) only install a container's default route when its primary attachment actually has a gateway -- on a gateway-less network a container gets none at all, exactly like a real host plugged into a real switch with no DHCP; the pre-existing `--route=0.0.0.0/0:VIA` mechanism, unchanged, remains how an operator points a container at a non-host-owned gateway (a VRRP address, for instance). `registry_alloc_ip()` gained an `exclude_be` parameter so a network's gateway -- no longer always host-part 1 -- can still be excluded from allocation correctly wherever it actually sits. **Migration**: a network persisted before this shipped has no `has_gateway` key in its JSON at all; that specific absence is the reliable signal to load it exactly as today's legacy behavior (`has_gateway=true`, `gateway=base|1`), never silently demoting an already-deployed network to gateway-less -- only entries written by the current code can describe a genuinely gateway-less network.

**VLAN + physical-NIC bridge attachment, one mechanism (ADR-0038).** `network_attach_interface(net_name, ifname, vlan_id)` (`daemon/src/network.c`): `vlan_id` 0 enslaves `ifname` directly to the network's bridge via the existing, unchanged `rtnl_link_set_master()` -- confirmed fully generic, no veth-specific assumptions, so a real physical NIC needed no new rtnetlink primitive at all for this half. A nonzero `vlan_id` instead creates a new `<ifname>.<vlan_id>` 802.1q sub-interface (new `rtnl_vlan_create()`, reusing the existing generic nested-attribute helpers) and enslaves *that*, leaving the parent NIC free for other VLANs on other networks -- chosen over bridge VLAN filtering specifically because it fits this project's existing one-bridge-per-network model with zero new per-port-tag concept the codebase doesn't already have. Validation reuses `--interface=`'s own single source of truth (`device_find()`); `enumerate_net_one()` (`daemon/src/device.c`) gained one addition -- an interface with a `/sys/class/net/<ifname>/master` symlink present is now `assignable=0`, the same sysfs-visibility-is-exclusivity pattern ADR-0022 already established for netns-moved interfaces, extended to cover bridge enslavement. New REST: `POST`/`DELETE /v1/networks/{name}/interfaces[/{ifname}]`; CLI: `network attach-interface`/`detach-interface`.

Fixing three existing tests' own now-broken assumptions, found live while regression-testing, not anticipated up front: `test_dns.c`, `test_daemon_net.c`, and `test_container_restart.c` each create a network with no explicit gateway and then either `dig`/`connect()` from the *host* straight at a container's IP, or (for restart's own readiness checks, `do_readiness_check()`/`wait_for_tcp_ready()` in `daemon/src/main.c`) have the *daemon itself* do so -- both need the host to have a real route into that subnet, which only an explicit `--gateway=` still provides under the new default. Fixed by giving each of those three tests' own networks an explicit gateway matching their real intent (host/daemon-side reachability), not by changing the new default itself. A genuinely separate, real characteristic of this specific sandbox surfaced during that same investigation: a `connect()` to a closed port on a Kanxeo-managed container's IP here takes several real seconds to fail rather than an instant refusal -- confirmed as a property of this nested-LXC environment itself (not a regression from this phase's own code, and not new: it would have applied identically before this phase, just never pushed any existing test's own timing close enough to a limit to surface), so `test_container_restart.c` genuinely needs a longer wall-clock budget than a quick interactive re-run allows, though it completes correctly (readiness timeouts still bound and give up as designed) given that budget.

Verified: full clean rebuild, zero warnings. New/changed test coverage -- `test_networks.c` (default gateway-less network, explicit `--gateway=` end-to-end, and a hand-spliced old-format persisted entry proving the migration path loads as legacy `has_gateway=true`), `test_container_net.c` (a new scenario, direct against each container's own `/proc/<pid>/net/route`: `has_gateway=0` installs no default route, `has_gateway=1` does, an explicit `0.0.0.0/0` route_spec still does regardless), `test_rtnetlink.c` (`rtnl_vlan_create()`/`rtnl_link_clear_master()` proven directly against a real veth-pair stand-in -- the sub-interface, not its parent, becomes the bridge port), new `test_network_interfaces.c` (every validation path over real HTTP: unknown ifname, a real-but-virtual veth correctly rejected the same way `GET /v1/devices` already excludes it, unknown network, out-of-range `vlan_id`, detach-never-attached) -- each 3 consecutive clean runs. Full pre-existing non-QEMU regression suite (21 binaries, including this phase's own new one) re-run clean.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** the full positive "attach a real assignable physical interface, confirm `GET /v1/devices` flips it to `assignable: false`, VLAN sub-interface genuinely carries traffic" path needs real hardware this sandbox doesn't have (the identical boundary ADR-0022 already documents -- every software-creatable interface this sandbox could make for itself is excluded from `net:` discovery by construction, not just bad luck) and is left as an explicit, known gap rather than faked with a stand-in that can't actually exercise it; bridge VLAN filtering was never built, a considered non-choice rather than a gap, since nothing in this project's topology needs one bridge multiplexing several VLANs' worth of segments over shared ports.

## Phase 23 (done): a real router recipe set -- bird, keepalived, iproute2, ipset, iptables, iputils, bash

Raised directly by the user, the natural payoff of Phase 22's own gateway-less networking: a router container needs real tools to actually route (bird, already existed), fail over a shared VRRP address between a router pair (keepalived, new), and manipulate the kernel's own networking/filtering state (`ip`, iproute2, already existed; `ipset`/`iptables`, new), plus `ping`/`arping`/`tracepath` for diagnostics (iputils, new) and a real shell to orchestrate them (bash, already existed). Four new recipes -- `keepalived.recipe`, `ipset.recipe`, `iptables.recipe`, `iputils.recipe` -- verified the same two-phase way every prior recipe in this set has been: a real local build first, then a full install through the actual `kanxeod` pipeline, ending with a real running container exercising the actual kernel functionality (not just `--version`).

Every real upstream in this batch (`netfilter.org`, `github.com/iputils/iputils`) 403s non-browser User-Agents, the same issue bird.recipe's own header comment already documented for a different project -- Debian's own `.orig.tar.*` repackaging (unmodified upstream, by Debian's own policy) sidesteps it uniformly across all four, each checksum verified two ways: against Debian's own published `.dsc` `Checksums-Sha256`, and independently by downloading and computing sha256 directly.

**A real, load-bearing constraint drove several build choices, not guessed at, confirmed against this build host's own real library versions**: `ipset` unconditionally requires libmnl (no graceful degradation, unlike iproute2's own optional use of it); `iptables` was built `--disable-nftables` because this host's own `libnftnl-dev` (1.2.4) is genuinely older than the >=1.2.6 that release's nftables-compat support requires -- the traditional xtables-legacy backend built instead is a real, complete, still-widely-deployed mode, not a degraded one. `keepalived` needed no flags at all -- every optional integration it probes for (OpenSSL, libnl-3, libiptc, libnftnl+libmnl) was genuinely present once this build host had the right `-dev` packages installed, so it's a real, full-featured build. `iputils` needed `-DBUILD_MANS=false` (a missing `xsltproc`, found empirically, not worth adding a whole docs toolchain for man pages no Kanxeo image ships anyway) and `-DNO_SETCAP_OR_SUID=true` (every container already runs its own cmd as real root, so `ping`'s own setcap/setuid dance is meaningless here).

Confirmed by direct code reading before writing any of these: `pkg_depends` sequences install order and target-image membership, but a package's own *build* container's lowerdir is always the shared toolchain image, never the target image's current content -- so a C library dependency (libmnl, libnftnl, libnl-3, ...) has to live in the *toolchain* itself to be buildable against at all, not solved by depending on a sibling recipe. Real `-dev` packages (`libmnl-dev`, `libnftnl-dev`, `libnfnetlink-dev`, `libnl-3-dev`, `libnl-genl-3-dev`, `libiptc-dev`, `libipset-dev`, `libcap-dev`, `libidn2-dev`, plus `meson`/`ninja-build` for iputils' own build system) were installed on this build host directly -- the exact same "install real tooling on the host, it flows into the toolchain for free via the existing wholesale `/usr` copy" precedent already established for the Rust/Go toolchains. Each recipe stages its own real extra runtime shared-library dependencies in `pkg_install()` (confirmed via `ldd` against each real build, not assumed) -- `pkg_seed_image_runtime()`'s global bootstrap set only ever covered the bare minimum every binary needs (ld.so/libc/libtinfo/libgcc_s/libm), never a per-package transitive closure.

**A real, generic platform bug found live, not anticipated**: `merge_tree()` (`daemon/src/pkg.c`, the function that copies a package's built output into its target image) silently dropped every symlink -- a documented "v1 boundary" from when it was first written, never exercised because no recipe before `iptables.recipe` had actually installed one. iptables' own `make install` creates `iptables`/`ip6tables`/`iptables-save`/... as symlinks to a single `xtables-legacy-multi` binary; the first real end-to-end install produced a container image with the real binary present but every one of those command names missing. Fixed properly, not worked around in the recipe: `merge_tree()` gained a real `S_ISLNK` branch (`readlink()` the source, `symlink()` it at the destination, `unlink()`-before-`symlink()` for upgrade-safety, tracked in the install manifest exactly like a regular file) -- `unlink_manifest_files()`'s existing plain `unlink()` already removes a symlink correctly, so no separate removal-path change was needed. The same real end-to-end verification also caught `iproute2.recipe` (written in an earlier phase) silently gaining new optional features -- and new, previously-unstaged runtime library dependencies (`libelf`, `libmnl`, `libcap`, `libz`) -- purely because this phase's own toolchain additions made those optional dependencies newly detectable; fixed by extending that recipe's own `pkg_install()` the same way every other recipe in this set already stages its real extras.

Verified end-to-end against a live daemon: all seven packages installed for real onto a `router` image through the actual `kanxeod` pipeline; every binary confirmed to actually run (`--version`/equivalent) via direct chroot into the merged image; then, inside a real running container with a real network attachment, genuine kernel-level proof, not just process-starts-successfully -- a real `iptables -A INPUT ...` rule inserted and confirmed present via `iptables -L`, a real `ipset create`/`ipset add`/`ipset list` round-trip against the kernel's own ipset state, `ip addr show` confirming the container's real interface/address. Full clean rebuild, zero warnings; full pre-existing non-QEMU regression suite (21 binaries) re-run clean after the `merge_tree()` change.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** no base-image convention for standard FHS runtime directories (`/run`, in particular -- `iptables`' own `/run/xtables.lock` locking needs it to exist, confirmed missing on a minimal from-scratch image with no other reason to create it) -- a real, generic gap this phase's own verification surfaced but didn't attempt to solve, since it's a base-image/filesystem-layout concern shared by many future packages, not something any one recipe should own; no VRRP/bird/keepalived topology has actually been wired up and proven end-to-end yet (the two-router-plus-VRRP-address scenario that originally motivated Phase 22) -- this phase proves the tools are real and functional individually, not that topology as a whole.

## Phase 24 (done): the actual VRRP + bird topology, proven end-to-end

The payoff of Phase 22 (gateway-optional networking) and Phase 23 (real router binaries): two router containers, a real VRRP-shared address as the segment's actual gateway (never the host), real OSPF between them, and a real client whose traffic survives one router going down without any configuration change on its own end -- the exact scenario that started the whole networking-redesign conversation, now genuinely built and verified, not just made possible.

Four containers, all `--image=router`, two new gateway-less networks (`lan1` 172.91.1.0/24, `wan1` 172.91.2.0/24): `cr-1`/`cr-2` multi-homed on both with `--ip-forward`, `keepalived.conf`/`bird.conf` staged via `--file=`; `blah` on `lan1` with `--route=0.0.0.0/0:172.91.1.254` (the VRRP address, never a router's own IP); `srv1` on `wan1` as the "far side" target. `--network=` attachment order confirmed empirically (not assumed) to control interface naming (`eth0`=first, `eth1`=second) before any config referenced an interface by name; `bird -c <file>` (no `-f`) daemonizes on its own, so `cmd` is a plain shell one-liner starting bird then `exec`ing `keepalived -n -f <file> -l` as the container's real PID 1.

**Three more real, generic gaps found live, on top of Phase 23's own `/run` finding, all from the same root cause -- no image built by this platform ships any baseline FHS layout at all, not even `/dev`:**
- `bird` hard-fails immediately (`Cannot open /dev/null`) with no `/dev/null` present -- confirmed by watching it crash-loop the whole container via `restart:"always"` until fixed. Standard `/dev/{null,zero,full,random,urandom}` character device nodes (major:minor `1:3`/`1:5`/`1:7`/`1:8`/`1:9`, the exact same convention `test_image_fixture_stage_toolchain()`'s own `dev_nodes[]` already uses for the toolchain image) staged directly into the `router` image.
- `birdc`/`birdcl` need `/usr/var/run` to exist for their control socket (`bird`'s own `--prefix=/usr`-relative default, confirmed via its real `--help`) -- same fix, a plain `mkdir -p`.
- `bird.recipe` itself (from an earlier phase) never staged `libreadline.so.8` -- `birdc`'s own interactive client links it (confirmed via `ldd`), never exercised until this phase's own verification actually ran it; `birdcl` (no-readline variant) doesn't need it, confirmed as a real, complete substitute for scripted/one-shot queries. Fixed in the recipe itself, the same way every other recipe in this set stages its own real extras.

These three fixes were applied directly to the already-built `router` image's own files (real, but not reproducible by a fresh `pkg install` from a clean image) -- a real, stated boundary, not silently glossed over: a from-scratch image build today still needs this same manual step until a real base-image FHS-layout convention exists (the gap Phase 23 already named for `/run`, now confirmed broader).

**A real design lesson, not anticipated in the plan**: the first deployment attempt gave `blah` a VRRP-protected default route on `lan1` but left `srv1` on `wan1` pointed at nothing -- `srv1` had no route back to `lan1`'s subnet at all, so ping requests arrived (proving forwarding worked one-way) but replies had nowhere to go (100% loss, confirmed and diagnosed step-by-step: `blah`→router direct reachability fine, router→`srv1` direct reachability fine, only the full round trip failed). Fixed by making VRRP genuinely symmetric -- a second `vrrp_instance` on `eth1`/`wan1` (its own `virtual_router_id`, its own virtual IP `172.91.2.254`) alongside the existing one on `eth0`/`lan1`, with `srv1` given an explicit static route back to `lan1` via that second address. This is the more correct design anyway (both segments get real redundancy, not just the client-facing one) -- a genuine improvement the failure itself surfaced, not a workaround.

Verified end-to-end, in order: VRRP election (`cr-1`, priority 150, owns both `.254` addresses; `cr-2`, priority 100, owns neither); OSPF adjacency reaches genuine `Full` state on both sides (`birdcl show ospf neighbors`: `Full/DR` on one, `Full/BDR` on the other); real packet forwarding (`blah` pings `srv1`, 0% loss, through `cr-1`'s real forwarding path); **the actual payoff** -- stopping `cr-1` (`kanxeoctl stop`, which stays stopped, confirmed distinct from a crash-restart) makes `cr-2` take over both VRRP addresses within seconds, and `blah` keeps reaching `srv1` at 0% loss throughout, with `blah`'s own route table (checked before and after) never changing at all; restarting `cr-1` reclaims MASTER via keepalived's default preemption, steady state returns, OSPF adjacency re-confirmed healthy.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** no base-image FHS-layout convention (the `/dev`/`/run`/`/usr/var/run` gap named above) -- a real, generic, still-open item; no VRRP authentication configured (VRRPv3 doesn't support it at all per RFC 5798, and this is a verification deployment, not a hardening exercise -- a real, stated boundary); OSPF's own dynamic route learning isn't load-bearing in this specific topology (both routers are already directly, statically connected to both segments) -- genuine adjacency-forming is real proof bird's implementation works on this platform, but a topology where OSPF-learned routes are actually *required* for forwarding (three-plus routers, asymmetric connectivity) is a natural, valuable future extension, not attempted here.

## Phase 25 (done): diagnose real-install PKI/pkg bootstrap failures, fix silent error paths

Raised directly by the user against their own real booted install (QEMU or bare metal, not this dev sandbox): the web dashboard's "Bootstrap CA" button failed with the generic "PKI operation failed", and "Bootstrap build image" failed with the generic "package operation failed" -- both HTTP 500s carrying no real diagnostic. Two separate problems, traced independently rather than guessed at.

**Problem 1: `respond_pki_error()`/`respond_pkg_error()` (`daemon/src/main.c`) collapse several distinct enum values into one generic 500 string each -- but tracing what actually sits behind those enum values surfaced a real, load-bearing gap: `persist_mkdir_p()`/`kx_mkdir_p()` (`include/pathutil.h`), `persist_atomic_write()`, and most of `persist_read_file()`'s own failure branches (`daemon/src/persist.c`) failed completely silently, no `fprintf`/`perror` at all -- unlike `pki.c`'s own `openssl` subprocess failures, which already print a real reason to stderr. `persist_mkdir_p(g_pki_dir)` is the literal first thing `pki_ca_create()` does, before any `openssl` invocation; a failure there today produces zero diagnostic anywhere, console included. Confirmed the same class of silence in `pkg.c`'s `run_subprocess()` (an `execve()` failure or nonzero child exit was never reported, only turned into a bare -1) and its near-duplicate in `test/test_image_fixture.c`'s `run_cp_a()`. Fixed at the shared primitive level, not per call site -- `persist.c`/`pathutil.h` are used by `pki.c`, `pkg.c`, `network.c`, `dns.c`, `image.c`, `containerdef.c`, and `main.c` alike, so logging added once there (naming the exact path and `strerror(errno)`, or the exact exit status/signal for subprocess failures) benefits every subsystem for free -- One Source of Truth, not eight copies of the same `fprintf`. Verified in isolation (a throwaway harness pointed `kx_mkdir_p`/`persist_atomic_write` at `/proc/...`, a guaranteed-unwritable path) that the new diagnostics correctly name the failing path and reason.

**Problem 2: the web dashboard's "Bootstrap build image" button (`web/app.js`) always POSTed an empty body to `/v1/pkg/bootstrap`**, meaning it could only ever use `pkg_bootstrap_build_image()` -- the dev-convenience fallback that copies the toolchain live from `kanxeod`'s own host, explicitly documented (Phase 20) as producing an empty, non-functional toolchain on a real minimal install. The correct production path, `pkg_bootstrap_from_toolchain(toolchain_path)`, already existed server-side and via the CLI's own `--toolchain=` flag (Phase 20) -- the dashboard simply had no field for it. Fixed: `web/index.html` gains an optional "Toolchain artifact path" input plus an explanatory hint (blank stays today's dev-fallback behavior byte-for-byte); `web/app.js` includes it as `toolchain_path` in the POST body only when non-empty.

**Decisive verification, two layers.** First layer: rather than guess whether Phase 20's own fixes (staging `openssl`+libs+config onto the control-plane root, correcting `pkg_seed_image_runtime()`'s source paths) were actually still intact, ran both of that phase's own permanent QEMU-based regression tests -- `test/test_console_pki_bootstrap.c` and `test/test_console_pkg_bootstrap.c` -- fresh, against current source with the new logging in place.
