Both PASS cleanly on this dev sandbox: `pki ca bootstrap` over the real console produces a real, valid CA certificate; `pkg bootstrap`'s toolchain-staging self-test confirms a real, working `gcc`. Second, stronger layer: built a real, distributable installer `.iso` from that same current source (`build/mkbootroot` + `build/mkinstalleriso`, the project's own real signing keys, the same tools and artifact shape an operator actually uses -- not a test-only shortcut) and handed it to the user for a genuine fresh install on their own real environment. **The user confirmed all three commands succeed there**: `pki ca bootstrap`, `pkg bootstrap`, and `pki cert create --name=test.local` (a full leaf-cert issuance, not just CA creation). This closes the loop for real -- not just "current source should work," but "current source does work, on the user's own real install." Confirms the diagnosis: their original install predated Phase 20's fixes; there was no live regression in current source to chase further.

Full clean rebuild, zero warnings. No local regression suite run against `daemon/src/persist.c`'s shared-state test binaries (`test_pki`, `test_pkg`, etc.) this phase -- a live dev-sandbox `kanxeod` instance was running the real Phase 24 VRRP topology (`cr-1`/`cr-2`/`srv1`/`blah`) against the shared default `/var/lib/kanxeo` state directory at the time, and those test binaries reset that exact directory (the same cross-process clobbering hazard noted in Phases 23-24); the two QEMU console tests above, each booting its own disposable disk image, gave equally decisive proof without that risk.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** the real base-image FHS-layout convention itself (Phases 23-24's own "Not built" item, re-confirmed still open) -- this phase only made failures in that space, and everywhere else, loud instead of silent; it did not build the convention. Also not done: surfacing the now-much-better console diagnostics through the REST API response itself (`respond_pki_error()`/`respond_pkg_error()` still return the same generic strings to API clients) -- deliberately out of scope, since exposing internal error detail over the API is a separate design question (info-leak surface on a REST API) the user didn't ask to open.

## Phase 26 (done): package recipes are a real, live-managed catalog -- a fresh install had none at all, and updating one shouldn't need a reinstall

Raised directly by the user, live, while walking through creating their first test container on the freshly-installed box from Phase 25: `pkg install --name=bash` failed with "no such recipe, or it failed to parse". Investigation found a real, total gap, not specific to bash -- no real install has ever had *any* `*.recipe` file staged anywhere. Every recipe that "worked" on this project's own dev sandbox got there by a developer manually copying files from the repo into `/var/lib/kanxeo/pkg/recipes` across earlier sessions, never a real platform mechanism.

**First attempt (ADR-0039, superseded by ADR-0040):** mirrored ADR-0019's own mechanism verbatim -- stage recipes into the installer's payload, copy them onto the containers partition at real install time. It worked (verified via a full real Secure-Boot QEMU install), but the user immediately, correctly pushed back: that ties every recipe addition or update to a full ISO rebuild and OS reinstall. Runtime libs (what ADR-0019 actually seeds) are fixed infrastructure that basically never changes; recipes are a package catalog, expected to grow continuously, the way `apt update` never requires reinstalling Debian. Mirroring ADR-0019's *mechanism* without checking whether recipes share its *nature* was the mistake. Reverted in full (`mkinstalleriso.c`/`kanxeo-install.c`/`test_installer.c`/`README.md` back to byte-for-byte their pre-ADR-0039 shape, confirmed via clean recompile) rather than left half in place.

**The real fix (ADR-0040):** recipes are managed live, over the REST API, on an already-running system -- `POST /pkg/recipes` (upsert by name; `content` validated with the exact same `parse_recipe()` every install already uses, including the "name must equal content's own `pkg_name=`" invariant, *before* anything on disk changes, so an invalid upload can never clobber a recipe that already worked) and `DELETE /pkg/recipes/{name}`. CLI: `kanxeoctl pkg recipe add --name=NAME --file=PATH` / `pkg recipe rm NAME`. `test/test_image_fixture.c`'s `test_image_fixture_copy_dir_files()` (extracted from `mkbootroot.c` during the first attempt) stays regardless -- it's still genuinely used for `web/`/firmware staging, independent of the recipes question.

Verified three ways: full clean rebuild, zero warnings; an isolated harness (its own scratch `pkg_init()`, no shared daemon state) proving the core add/delete logic directly -- invalid name, name/`pkg_name=` mismatch, malformed content, and a *bad* upsert all rejected with nothing written or clobbered on disk; a valid add and a valid upsert both succeed; delete removes the file; delete-of-missing 404s; `test/test_pkg.c` gaining a full HTTP-level scenario covering the same ground plus the actual payoff -- a recipe added purely through the API installs for real (fetch, build, merge into the base image, binary present) exactly like a hand-written recipe file already does.

Also verified live, at the user's own request, against this sandbox's real daemon -- restarted it to pick up the new binary. A real, load-bearing gap in my own reasoning surfaced doing that, not anticipated: each container is armed with `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG SIGKILL` (`src/container.c`), so killing the daemon kills every container immediately, not leaving them running as orphans the way I'd predicted beforehand -- wrong, and told to the user as soon as it was confirmed wrong, not glossed over. What actually saved the real Phase 24 VRRP topology (`cr-1`/`cr-2`/`srv1`/`blah`) already running on this sandbox: the daemon's own startup path relaunches every persisted `restart:"always"` container definition, not just crash-restarting an already-tracked one -- confirmed directly (fresh PIDs, `keepalived`'s own startup banner in the new daemon's log, `kanxeoctl ps` showing all four running again) and, more importantly, confirmed *correct*, not just *present*: `cr-1` genuinely re-won VRRP MASTER and owns both `.254` addresses again (`ip addr` inside its own netns via `nsenter`), the same real election Phase 24 originally proved, not just four processes that happen to exist. The recipe API itself then verified live on top of that same restarted daemon: add → list → rm → rm-again-404, plus the `respond_pkg_recipe_error()` wording fix below, all confirmed against real HTTP responses, not just the isolated harness.

**A genuine, if small, correctness fix found by this live testing, not anticipated:** `respond_pkg_error()` is shared across every `pkg_error`-returning endpoint, but its wording ("no such package", "invalid package name") reads wrong for a recipe operation -- `DELETE /pkg/recipes/{name}` on a name that was never added returned "no such package (HTTP 404)", genuinely confusing. Fixed with a small, dedicated `respond_pkg_recipe_error()` (same enum, recipe-appropriate wording: "no such recipe", "invalid recipe name", etc.), used only by the two new recipe handlers -- every other `pkg_error` caller is untouched.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** a fresh install still starts with zero recipes -- a deliberate trade-off now (see ADR-0040), not a gap: the first real action on a new box is `pkg recipe add`, not everything pre-baked whether needed or not. `POST /v1/system/restore`'s own `pkg_recipes` field remains a valid bulk-seeding path for an operator who wants to restore a whole catalog at once. The real base-image FHS-layout convention itself remains open, unaffected by this phase.

## Phase 27 (done): a real container-image baseline FHS layout -- the last open item from the diagnostic arc

Closes the specific gap Phase 23 (`iptables`'s `/run/xtables.lock`) and Phase 24 (`bird` crashing with no `/dev/null`, `birdc`'s control socket needing a directory that didn't exist) both hit and both fixed by hand, directly on the already-built `router` image, explicitly flagged both times as not reproducible from a fresh `pkg install`. See ADR-0041.

Reused, rather than reinvented, the existing extension point: `daemon/src/pkg.c`'s `pkg_seed_image_runtime()` -- already idempotent, already called both at `image_create()` (new images) and after every `pkg_build_completed()` (so any existing image self-heals the next time anyone installs anything into it, no migration needed) -- renamed to `pkg_seed_image_baseline()` (its scope is genuinely broader now) and extended with the standard `/dev/{null,zero,full,random,urandom}` char device nodes (the exact table/pattern `test/test_image_fixture.c`'s own toolchain-image staging already proves safe in this sandbox) and a plain, empty `/run` directory -- no tmpfs mount needed, confirmed OverlayFS already makes a shared-lowerdir directory safe (anything a container writes there copies up into its own private upperdir). Unlike the runtime-lib loop's tolerant-if-source-missing handling, a real `mknod`/`mkdir` failure here has no such excuse and is fatal.

**`pkg/recipes/bird.recipe` fixed at its actual root, verified empirically, not assumed**: fetched bird's real source, ran `autoreconf -fi && ./configure --help`, then grepped `configure.ac`/`Makefile.in` and confirmed directly `CONTROL_SOCKET="$(runstatedir)/bird.ctl"` -- bird's own bare `--prefix=/usr` (no `--runstatedir=` override) was the real cause of the `/usr/var/run` gap, not a generic missing-`/run` problem. Added `--runstatedir=/run` to the recipe's `pkg_build()`; a real local `./configure && make` confirmed the resulting binary's compiled-in `PATH_CONTROL_SOCKET` is genuinely `/run/bird.ctl` (checked the actual generated header and ran `strings` against the real built binary, not inferred from `configure`'s own reported variable alone).

Verified: full clean rebuild, zero warnings. `test/test_pkg.c`'s existing per-image "step 13" scenario (installs `greeter@router` into a freshly-created `router` image, already proving runtime-lib seeding) extended with the same-style `stat()` checks for all 5 dev nodes and `/run` -- the one existing scenario that actually proves per-image, first-install seeding, no new scenario needed.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** still a fixed, hardcoded baseline set, not an extensible "package declares what extra baseline content it needs" mechanism -- same shape ADR-0019's runtime-lib set already is. `/tmp` deliberately not staged into container images (a considered decision: the toolchain image's own separate `/tmp` staging serves a different purpose, and no container-image failure has ever named a missing `/tmp`). No `/var/run -> /run` compat symlink (would not have fixed bird's own real `/usr/var/run` gap anyway, and nothing has hit a missing `/var/run`). Scoped to container images only -- the control-plane root needs none of this (`CONFIG_DEVTMPFS` already covers `/dev` there; `kanxeod`'s own state never touches `/run`).

## Phase 28 (done): the installer relays interactive I/O through a PTY across both consoles at once

Raised directly by the user: the installer's own GRUB kernel command line already requests both `/dev/tty0` and `/dev/ttyS0`, but Linux binds `/dev/console` to whichever `console=` is listed *last* -- today, `ttyS0` -- so an operator watching the video console (the common case on real hardware/Proxmox) sees nothing at all during install, including `fdisk`'s own interactive partitioning session and `mokutil`'s Secure Boot enrollment password prompt. Offered a one-line kernel-arg reorder (flips which single console works) against real, simultaneous dual-console support, the user chose the latter. See ADR-0042.

`kanxeod`'s own dual-console mechanism (an independent forked process per tty, `daemon/src/main.c`'s `spawn_console_shell()`) doesn't transfer to the installer -- `kanxeo-install` is a linear, single-pass sequence, not a daemon hosting independent sessions, so a different mechanism was needed: relay *one* child's I/O across both consoles at once. New `image/src/dual_console.c`/`.h` -- the first PTY usage anywhere in this codebase -- extracted into its own module specifically so a new, fully-automated `test/test_dual_console.c` could link and exercise the *real* relay logic directly (no QEMU, two throwaway PTYs standing in for the two real consoles), not a re-implementation of it. `run_subprocess_dual_console()` replaces the `fdisk`/`mokutil` call sites only; `dual_printf()`/`dual_perror()` replace every status/error message in `kanxeo-install.c` so the installer's own narration reaches both consoles too, not just its two interactive subprocesses.

**A genuinely new mount, found during design**: `posix_openpt()`'s slave device only resolves once `devpts` is mounted -- nothing else in this from-scratch PID1 environment ever did that. `early_mounts()` now mounts it, same tolerance posture as its existing `proc`/`sysfs` mounts. The kernel option gating this (`CONFIG_UNIX98_PTYS`) turned out to already be compiled into the exact kernel this whole session has been testing against, via `make olddefconfig`'s own default resolution rather than an explicit line in the kernel config fragment -- added explicitly now for honest, reproducible documentation; no kernel rebuild was needed to prove the design.

Verified two ways: `test/test_dual_console.c` (a forked "driver" process plays `kanxeo-install`'s own role against two self-created PTYs; the test itself plays "two operators," driving input into first one console then the other and confirming the echo lands on *both* each time) -- 3 consecutive clean runs; `test/test_installer.c`'s existing real 5-session Secure-Boot QEMU flow (already scripts a real interactive `fdisk` session and a real `mokutil` password prompt) re-run clean through the new code -- full PASS, no regression on the one console (`ttyS0`) this sandbox's own harness can actually automate.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** real video-console output stays an explicit, honest, user-owned gap -- this sandbox's QEMU harness has no video backend at all and cannot scrape text off one, the same already-accepted boundary Phase 14's own video-console driver work has. The two-PTY test proves the relay mechanism is correct; it cannot prove a real `/dev/tty0` device behaves identically to a PTY slave (no reason to expect otherwise, but not empirically checked here) -- the user's own real-hardware/Proxmox confirmation is the necessary final check.

## Phase 29 (done): Proxmox-style web dashboard redesign -- left resource tree, per-resource detail views

Raised directly by the user: the dashboard (`web/index.html`/`app.js`/`style.css`) was a flat column of 9 form-and-table panels with no navigation, and several real, already-shipped REST capabilities had zero UI at all -- Images, Devices, System (backup/restore/update/reboot/shutdown), network interface attach/detach, recipe add/delete, container Stop-without-remove. The "Run a container" form itself exposed only about half of what `POST /v1/containers` actually accepts. The user wanted something closer to Proxmox -- a left-side resource tree, compute wired to hardware/networks/storage -- confirmed via three explicit design-fork answers: stay framework-free (ADR-0010 reconfirmed, not reopened), a left-side resource tree (not tabs or a different shape), and one full redesign pass rather than an incremental rollout.

**Two honest scope boundaries, stated up front and not silently glossed over**: no live resource-usage monitoring exists in the API (no CPU/RAM graphs, no `/v1/stats` -- Proxmox's own live "Summary" charts have no backing endpoint here), and no virtual-disk/volume concept exists (storage is OverlayFS -- one shared read-only image plus a private per-container upperdir, not attachable disks -- so Proxmox's own disk/storage management has no analog to build UI for). Both are real Proxmox-analogy gaps with no existing REST endpoint; per the API-First Mandate, building UI for either would mean designing new backend capability first, out of scope for a UI-only redesign.

Full REST API surface traced against `docs/api/openapi.yaml` and `daemon/src/device.c`/`image.c` before designing anything, rather than working from memory -- this surfaced the Images/Devices/System gap and the container-create form's missing fields directly, as verified fact rather than assumption.

**Information architecture**: tree root implicit ("Kanxeo", single daemon, no cluster/node level), 8 category children -- Containers, Networks, Images, Devices, DNS (-> Records, Servers), PKI (-> Root CA, Certificates), Packages (-> Recipes, Installed), System (leaf). Clicking a category shows the existing summary-table-plus-create-form pattern (kept, since it already worked); clicking an individual resource (a container, network, or image) shows a real detail view -- the genuinely new content, since before this only the flat table row existed. Routing is `location.hash` (`#containers`, `#containers/{name}`, ...) driving a `renderCurrentView()` function via `hashchange` plus on load -- no router library, matching this project's own hand-rolled-over-dependency posture everywhere else. The tree and every summary table are rebuilt from the same already-polled data (the existing 2s `poll()` loop, extended to also fetch Images and Devices) -- one fetch, two renderings, not a second data path.

**Detail views, the actual new content**: container detail surfaces every field the API already returned but nothing showed before -- `devices[]`, `interfaces[]`, `files[]`, `sysctls{}`, `restart`/`restart_delay_seconds`, `depends_on[]`, `readiness` -- plus a real Stop action (`POST .../stop`, previously had zero UI anywhere) alongside Remove. Network detail adds attached-interfaces display with attach (`POST .../interfaces`, picking from `GET /devices`'s assignable `net` entries) and detach (`DELETE .../interfaces/{ifname}`) -- neither had UI before. Image detail is new entirely: cross-references already-polled container/package data client-side (no new endpoints needed) to show which containers use an image and which packages are installed into it, plus create/delete with real 400/409 error surfacing. Devices view groups by bus, with GPU member nodes (`gpu:0:card0`, `gpu:0:renderD128`, ...) grouped under their synthesized `gpu:N` prefix so the UI's own grouping matches exactly what a container's `devices: ["gpu:0"]` grant actually expands to server-side (`device_find_group()`) -- no attach action here, since devices are only ever granted at container-creation time, a real API boundary not a UI gap. System view adds backup download, restore upload, update (image/kernel path staging), and reboot/shutdown, the last two behind a real `confirm()` guard since both are genuinely destructive.

**Container creation stayed a single page**, reorganized into `<fieldset>` sections (Identity, Resources, Networking, Devices, Storage & files, Lifecycle, TLS) rather than a multi-step wizard -- at this resource's actual field count (~15 fields, not Proxmox's own much larger VM-creation surface), a wizard would mean hand-rolling step state/validation/back-forward navigation for no real benefit over a well-grouped scrolling form.

No daemon/CLI changes anywhere in this phase -- every capability the redesign surfaces already had a REST endpoint; this was a pure API-client change, consistent with the API-First Mandate.

Verified: full clean rebuild, zero warnings. Every DOM id `web/app.js` binds against was cross-checked directly against the actual `web/index.html` markup (not assumed from memory) -- containers table (7->5 columns), recipes table (3->4 columns), package-installed table (7->8 columns), every new detail-view/form field id, all confirmed to line up. Every endpoint path, request field, and response schema field `app.js` uses was cross-checked directly against `docs/api/openapi.yaml` (`ContainerCreateRequest`/`Container`/`Network`/`NetworkInterfaceAttachRequest`/`Device`/`PkgEntry`/`PkgRecipe`/system-update-response), including two easy-to-get-wrong details confirmed against the daemon's own source (`daemon/src/network.c`): `ContainerCreateRequest.interfaces` takes bare kernel names ("wlan0"), not "net:"-prefixed device ids, while `ContainerCreateRequest.devices` takes the id verbatim (or a `gpu:N` group prefix) -- the UI's device/interface pickers handle this distinction correctly. Exercised live against this sandbox's own already-running `kanxeod` (confirmed serving these exact files byte-for-byte, no restart needed to pick up a static-file change) and its real, live state (the Phase 24 VRRP topology plus test-created images/packages/certs) -- container/network/image/device/pkg listings from `curl` match the new renderers field-for-field, and the real 400/409 error bodies (`DELETE /images/base` -> "the base image cannot be removed"; `DELETE /images/router` -> "image is still referenced by a running container") surface through the existing `apiRequest()`/`showStatus()` convention exactly as designed.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** interactive browser click-through (tree navigation via mouse, live form submission, watching real DOM updates) -- this sandbox has no browser or headless-browser tooling available, so verification here is build correctness plus live-data/schema cross-check, not a human click-test. The user's own browser session against the running daemon is the remaining, necessary final check, the same "sandbox can prove the mechanism, real environment confirms it" shape Phase 28's own video-console gap already has.

## Phase 30 part 1 (done): an interactive shell into a running container, over a hand-rolled WebSocket

Raised directly by the user while reviewing the redesigned dashboard: the tree now has a place to open a console into a running container, but no such capability existed anywhere -- only a host-physical-tty login shell (`spawn_console_shell()`) and an install-time PTY relay (ADR-0042), neither network-reachable nor container-aware. See ADR-0043.

Staged in 3 parts per the Iterative Workflow; this entry covers part 1 (the daemon mechanism itself, verified without any client UI yet) -- parts 2 (`kanxeoctl console`) and 3 (the web dashboard's own minimal terminal) are still ahead.

**Transport**: a minimal, hand-rolled RFC 6455 WebSocket (`daemon/src/websocket.c`/`.h`) -- no fragmentation support (every frame sent/accepted is a complete single-frame message, matching this endpoint's own keystroke/output traffic shape), payloads capped at 64KiB. `Sec-WebSocket-Accept` is computed by shelling to `/usr/bin/openssl` (`dgst -sha1 -binary | base64`), the same "never link crypto, always shell to the real `openssl` binary" convention `pki.c` already established -- verified against RFC 6455's own worked example. `daemon/src/http.c`'s `find_content_length()` was generalized into a reusable `http_find_header()` rather than writing a second, parallel header scanner for `Upgrade`/`Connection`/`Sec-WebSocket-*`.

**Exec mechanism**: `daemon/src/exec.c`'s `exec_into_container()` joins an already-running container's mount/UTS/network/pid namespaces via `setns()` (no IPC namespace -- containers never isolate it) and execs a command against a PTY allocated in the daemon's own namespace *before* any `setns()` call -- the slave fd is simply inherited across `fork()`, so the exec'd process never needs a working `devpts` inside the container (confirmed containers have none today). **A real bug caught empirically, not by inspection**: opening all four namespace fds must happen *before* any `setns()` call, not interleaved one at a time -- the first `setns()` (mnt) moves the caller into the container's own mount namespace immediately, so opening the *later* fds by path afterward resolves against the container's own (often proc-less) `/proc`, not the host's. Hit this exactly (`ENOENT` opening `ns/uts` right after `ns/mnt`'s own `setns()` had already succeeded) against a real running container while building this, before fixing the ordering. Default exec command is `/usr/bin/bash`, not `/bin/sh` -- also confirmed empirically: this project's own package system stages everything under `usr/bin/`, with no `/bin` directory at all on any real image.

**New `GET /v1/containers/{name}/console`** (`docs/api/openapi.yaml`, documented as an Upgrade-required GET). `daemon/src/main.c`'s `try_console_upgrade()` repurposes an accepted connection in place (`CONN_CLIENT` -> `CONN_CONSOLE_WS`) instead of the usual dispatch-then-close-the-fd cycle, pairing it with a new `CONN_CONSOLE_PTY` connection for the exec'd shell's pty master. **A new correctness hazard, solved deliberately**: this is the first time the daemon has needed two independently-epoll-registered fds representing one logical session that can tear each other down -- a single `epoll_wait()` batch can report both halves ready at once, so freeing the first one processed on the spot would leave the second event's own `struct conn *` dangling for the rest of that batch. Fixed with a small deferred-free queue (`g_pending_free`, drained once per batch) rather than freeing synchronously.

**Web dashboard's own terminal stays hand-rolled, not xterm.js** -- confirmed directly with the user: full ANSI/VT100 fidelity would need real terminal emulation, substantial to build from scratch, and vendoring a library would be a deliberate exception to ADR-0010's "no framework" constraint set just one phase earlier. The user chose to keep the web terminal minimal (reduced fidelity, no vim/top/less support) rather than open that exception; `kanxeoctl console` (part 2, not yet built) is the fully-capable path.

Verified in stages against real infrastructure, not mocks: `exec_into_container()` alone against this sandbox's own real, already-running `cr-1` router container (real shell arithmetic evaluating, not just PTY input echo, proving genuine execution); the full handshake+relay via both a manual raw-socket smoke test and the new permanent `test/test_console_exec.c` (5 consecutive clean runs) -- reuses `test/dual_console_child.c` (Phase 28's own trivial echo child) as the exec target rather than writing a near-duplicate. Confirmed no leaked exec'd process survives session teardown. Full clean rebuild, zero warnings.

**A real mistake, caught and fixed the same session, told plainly**: `test_console_exec.c`'s cleanup helper (matching every other daemon test's own `reset_state()` pattern) does `rm -rf` on `container_defs.json` -- this project's daemon has no test/production state isolation (`BASE_DIR` is hardcoded, confirmed no override flag exists), a pre-existing gap Phase 25/26 had already flagged in passing but which this session ran straight into: running the new test 5 times against this sandbox's own live daemon (serving the real `cr-1`/`cr-2`/`srv1`/`blah`/`keeper` containers) wiped their persisted definitions -- the running containers were unaffected, but a future daemon restart would have lost all 5. Caught immediately via a live-state check (not left for later), and fixed by reading each container's real `cmd`/file content straight out of its own still-running `/proc/<pid>/{cmdline,root}` and recreating all 5 through the real API -- confirmed byte-identical config, VRRP correctly re-electing `cr-1` as MASTER again afterward. **Lesson applied going forward**: no daemon test gets run again against this sandbox while its own production-like daemon is up on default paths, the same isolated-QEMU-test posture Phase 25 already chose for exactly this reason.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** parts 2 (`kanxeoctl console`) and 3 (web dashboard terminal) of this same phase; terminal resize (`SIGWINCH`/`TIOCSWINSZ`) propagation; any new authentication layer for this endpoint (exactly as protected as every other existing endpoint today, not a new gap this one introduces, but named plainly since it's a more sensitive capability than most).

## Phase 30 part 2 (done): `kanxeoctl console` -- a real, fully-interactive terminal client

New `client/src/console.c`/`.h` (kept separate from `httpclient.c`, same split rationale Phase 28's `dual_console.c` already established): hand-written WebSocket upgrade request over a raw socket (`kx_client_connect_raw()`, `httpclient.c`'s own internal `connect_to()` promoted to a public function rather than duplicated -- along with consolidating its `write_all()` into the shared `kx_write_all()` `include/iohelpers.h` already introduced in part 1), a masked client-frame writer (RFC 6455 requires client frames to be masked -- genuinely the mirror image of `daemon/src/websocket.c`'s own `ws_write_frame()`, which just as correctly never masks, since a server never does -- two small, deliberately different implementations for two different roles, not an accidental duplication), a tolerant unmasked-or-masked server-frame reader, and `cfmakeraw()` on the *local* terminal for the session's duration (the CLI's own first use of raw mode on its own terminal, distinct from `dual_console.c`'s "make the two *host* consoles I'm relaying through raw"). `cli/src/main.c` gains `kanxeoctl console NAME [--cmd=PATH]`.

**A real bug, caught by testing before it shipped**: the relay's `poll()` loop shrank `nfds` to 1 once local stdin hit EOF (to stop watching it) -- but `poll(2)` already ignores a negative fd entry on its own; shrinking `nfds` instead of just marking the entry `-1` meant `fds[1]` (the actual WebSocket socket) was no longer examined *at all*, hanging the whole session forever the moment stdin closed. Found immediately via a piped (non-tty) test session that hung, diagnosed with `strace` (confirmed the handshake and the first frame both sent correctly, then nothing -- the loop was blocked on a `poll()` that could structurally never return), fixed by always passing `nfds=2` and trusting `poll()`'s own negative-fd handling.

Verified two ways against this sandbox's own real, already-running `cr-1` container: a piped (non-raw-mode) session proving the handshake/relay/exit-detection logic on its own, and a real pty-backed session (Python's `pty.fork()`) exercising the full `cfmakeraw()` path end to end -- real bash prompt, real shell arithmetic evaluating (`$(( 21 * 2 ))` -> `42`), clean exit and terminal-restore message, 3 consecutive clean runs. Confirmed no leaked exec'd process and no impact on the container's own running state afterward. Full clean rebuild, zero warnings.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** part 3 (web dashboard terminal) of this same phase; terminal resize propagation (same open item as part 1 -- `kanxeoctl console` doesn't send `TIOCGWINSZ`/any resize notification to the daemon either, so the remote pty stays at its fixed default size even though the local terminal's own raw mode is otherwise fully set up for a real session).

## Phase 30 part 3 (done): the web dashboard's own console tab, a right-click context menu, and a Proxmox-style container view

Raised directly by the user, three related requests together: a right-click context menu on tree items for type-appropriate quick actions (start/stop/remove); clicking a resource showing its real configuration (already the Phase 29 detail-view pattern, kept); and, specifically for containers, a Proxmox-style layout -- a top action bar plus the main content defaulting to the **Console** itself, not a settings page behind a button. One real API gap surfaced and named rather than worked around: there is no way to restart an already-stopped, persisted container today (`GET /containers` never echoes back the original `cmd`, and there's no `POST .../start`, only re-`POST`ing the full original body) -- so the context menu offers Stop/Remove, not a "Start" that would have to be faked or silently broken.

**Terminal renderer** (`web/app.js`, `createTerminal()`): a line-buffer model, not a full VT100 emulator -- each line is an array of `{ch, style}` cells, written at `(cursorRow, cursorCol)`. Interprets `\r`/`\n`/backspace/Tab plus SGR color codes (`CSI ... m` -- bold, 8 standard + 8 bright foreground/background colors); every other CSI escape sequence (cursor addressing, clear-screen, etc.) is recognized structurally, by scanning to its final byte, so it's swallowed cleanly rather than leaking into the visible output as literal garbage text -- but not otherwise acted on, since there is no 2D cursor-addressable screen model here. This is the same accepted, stated boundary confirmed with the user back in part 1: full-screen redraw programs (vim, top, less) render wrong; `kanxeoctl console` (part 2) has no such limitation. Verified with a headless Node + minimal DOM-stub unit check (no real browser available in this sandbox) against a real captured bash transcript from earlier in this same phase (correct line splitting) and a synthetic SGR-plus-unsupported-CSI sequence (confirmed the red span renders with the right class, and `\x1b[2J\x1b[H` never appears as visible text).

**Container detail view restructured**: a `.detail-topbar` (title + Stop/Remove, always visible regardless of active tab) above a `.tab-bar` (Console / Summary), Console active by default. The browser's own native `WebSocket` object talks directly to `GET /v1/containers/{name}/console` (`ws://.../v1/containers/{name}/console`) -- no hand-rolled handshake needed client-side here, unlike the CLI's own console.c, since the browser already implements RFC 6455 itself. **A real re-render hazard, caught before it shipped**: the existing 2s poll loop calls `renderContainerDetail()` on every cycle to keep the Summary tab's fields live -- naively reopening the console's WebSocket on every one of those calls would have reset the session (lost input, flickering) every 2 seconds. Fixed with an `openConsole()` guard keyed on the currently-connected container's own name, a no-op if already connected to the same one; the connection is explicitly closed when navigating to a different container or away from the detail view entirely, so no session is ever left running invisibly in the background.

**Right-click context menu** (`#tree-context-menu`): event-delegated on the tree itself, type-aware per resource (`contextMenuItemsFor()`) -- Open console/Stop/Remove for containers, Remove for networks and images; categories and the fixed DNS/PKI/Packages/System/Devices nodes get no custom menu (the browser's own default one still works there). Reuses the exact same action functions (`stopContainer()`, `removeContainer()`, etc.) the buttons already call -- one implementation of each action, not a second copy for the menu.

Verified: the live daemon confirmed serving these exact files (no restart needed, this dashboard's own established fast-iteration setup); every new DOM id cross-checked between `app.js` and `index.html`; div/tag balance confirmed. Same honest boundary as parts 1/2's own web-facing verification and Phase 29's own redesign: no browser available in this sandbox, so this is data/logic/schema-level verification, not a human click-through -- the user's own browser session is the remaining, necessary final check.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** a real "start an already-stopped container" action (needs new backend work -- either the API echoing back `cmd`, or a dedicated `POST .../start` reading the persisted definition server-side -- neither exists today, named rather than faked); terminal resize propagation (same open item as parts 1/2).

**Same-day follow-up, two more UI refinements directly from the user**: a small colored status dot (`.tree-status-dot`) now sits before each container's name in the tree itself -- green for `running`, grey for `exited`, the only two states `Container.status` actually has (there is no "paused"/suspend state anywhere in this daemon, confirmed against the schema rather than assumed, so no third color was invented for one). And every category view's create form (Run a container, Create network/image/DNS record, Register DNS server, Issue certificate, Add recipe, Bootstrap build image, Install a package) is now a `<details>` element, collapsed by default -- clicking a category shows its list first, not every field a create action might need whether or not you're using it; the recipes view's own two separate actions (add-a-recipe, bootstrap-the-build-image) were also split into two independent `<details>`, matching how distinct they already were. Zero new JS logic needed for the collapse behavior itself -- native `<details>`/`<summary>`, no framework, consistent with ADR-0010.

**A second same-day follow-up, going further on both fronts, again directly from the user**: a Proxmox-style "+ Create" dropdown in the header's own top-right corner replaces the just-added collapsed inline forms entirely -- every creatable resource (Container, Network, Image, DNS Record, DNS Server, PKI Certificate, Package Recipe, Bootstrap Build Image, Install Package) is now one menu item opening a single shared modal (`#modal-overlay`), never a form sitting inline in a list view. The 8 existing forms weren't rewritten, only relocated -- each still lives in the page, in its own hidden `#modal-<form-id>` wrapper inside the modal's body; `openModal()` just un-hides the one that matches, and every existing submit handler's success path now also calls the new `closeModal()` -- except `pki-cert-form`, deliberately left open on success, since it's the one and only place a freshly-issued certificate's private key is ever shown and closing the modal would hide it before the operator could copy it.

The container detail view's own tab set grew from 2 (Console/Summary) to 5 -- **Summary** (status/image/pid/exit status only now), **Hardware** (devices/interfaces granted at creation, plus a newly-added Networks table that had no home in the old 2-tab layout at all), **Options** (restart policy/delay/depends_on/readiness/ip_forward, sysctls, files), **Console** (unchanged, still the default active tab), and **Backup**. The Hardware/Options split is a real, considered mapping, not an arbitrary one: `GET /containers` never echoes back `memory_max`/`pids_max` (confirmed against the schema, the same kind of gap already named for the "no start action" boundary), so Hardware only ever shows what the API actually returns -- devices, interfaces, network attachments -- not resource limits Proxmox's own Hardware tab would show. **Backup is an honest tab, not a working one**: there is no per-container backup/snapshot mechanism in this API today (OverlayFS has no snapshot concept, and `/system/backup` is a platform-config-wide dump, not workload data) -- confirmed directly with the user rather than assumed, the tab states this plainly and links to the System page's own real backup/restore instead of pretending a per-container capability exists. Tab-switching itself was generalized from two hardcoded panel ids to a `data-tab`-driven loop (`tab-panel.dataset.tab === tabName`), scaling to 5 panels with no special-casing.

Verified the same way as every other web-only change this phase: the live daemon confirmed serving these exact files (no restart needed); every one of the 101 distinct element ids `app.js` references cross-checked programmatically against `index.html` (all present exactly once, no orphans, no duplicates); `<div>`/`<section>`/`<form>` tag balance confirmed. Same honest boundary restated once more: no browser available in this sandbox, so this is structural/logic verification, not a human click-through -- the user's own browser session remains the final check.

**A real, significant bug the user's own browser session caught that every structural check above had missed, from the very start of Phase 29**: `[hidden]` alone does not reliably win against an author CSS rule setting `display` on the same selector -- normal-priority author rules always outrank the browser's own default `[hidden] { display: none }`, regardless of selector specificity, since cascade origin is compared before specificity. `.view { display: flex; ... }` has carried this exact defect since Phase 29's very first redesign, and `form { display: flex; ... }` inherited it the moment `#pki-ca-form`'s own visibility started being toggled directly via `.hidden` rather than through a wrapper -- meaning **every `.view` section has been rendering simultaneously, stacked down the page, this entire time**, regardless of which one `renderCurrentView()` actually set `.hidden = false` on; hash-based routing was still correctly tracking "which view is current" internally the whole time, it just never visually hid the others. Three screenshots from the user's own real browser (something this sandbox has never had) showed it directly: navigating to a container's own detail view rendered the full containers list, that container's 5-tab detail, the images list, an image detail view, the devices view, and the full packages list all stacked on one page. The freshly-added `.modal-overlay` had the identical defect (see this same day's earlier entry) -- fixing that one first, without immediately generalizing the lesson to every other selector sharing the same pattern, was a real miss; a systematic sweep (cross-referencing every CSS selector with an explicit `display` value against every element `app.js` actually toggles via `.hidden`) found the two remaining real instances (`.view`, `form`) and confirmed nothing else in the stylesheet has this shape. All four now carry an explicit `<selector>[hidden] { display: none; }` override -- `.modal-overlay`, `.tab-panel` (already fixed in part 3, is what tipped off the pattern), `.view`, `form`.

**This is the honest headline, not a footnote**: structural checks (tag balance, id cross-referencing, JS syntax) prove markup and wiring are self-consistent -- they cannot catch a rendering defect that only a real browser applying real CSS cascade rules exposes. Every "no browser in this sandbox, verified structurally" caveat threaded through Phases 29-30 was accurate about what was checked, but this bug is the concrete proof of why that boundary matters and why the user's own click-through was never optional busywork.

## Phase 30 part 4 (done): `kanxeod --data-dir=` for test/production state isolation

Triggered by a real incident during a full-suite regression sweep, not a designed-ahead feature: this project's own test suite had zero isolation from a live `kanxeod`'s state, and every daemon-linked test's own `reset_state()` shares this exact same hazard shape. Running the suite against this sandbox's own live daemon (a real 5-container VRRP/OSPF demo topology deployed during Phase 24) silently wiped its persisted `container_defs.json`/`networks.json`/`dns_records.json`, regenerated its PKI CA, and -- the most severe instance -- wiped the `router` image's entire installed-package rootfs and the daemon's own live recipe files, because every test daemon and every real daemon on the same host shared one hardcoded default path (`/var/lib/kanxeo`). This happened twice in the same session; both times required a full manual recovery (recreating networks/containers from captured live state via `/proc/<pid>/{cmdline,root}`, reinstalling all 7 `router` packages from source). See ADR-0044.

**Fix**: `kanxeod` gains `--data-dir=PATH` (default unchanged: `/var/lib/kanxeo`, zero behavior change for any existing invocation that omits it). Every subsystem already took its own state path as an explicit init-time parameter, confirmed by inspection before starting -- the fix was fully contained to `daemon/src/main.c`: the compile-time `BASE_DIR`/derived-macro block became a runtime `g_base_dir[PATH_MAX]` plus derived `static char [PATH_MAX]` path buffers, computed once by `init_base_dir_paths()` right after argv parsing, before `boot_init()` or any subsystem `_init()` call touches them. Two new derived paths (`PKG_RECIPES_DIR`, `PKI_CERTS_DIR`) replace call sites that previously relied on compile-time string-literal concatenation (`PKG_DIR "/recipes"`), which a runtime buffer can't do.

All 16 daemon-linked tests were updated to spawn their own `kanxeod` with `--data-dir=` pointed at a fresh directory created by a new shared helper (`test_data_dir_create()`/`test_data_dir_cleanup()`, `test/test_image_fixture.c` -- matching the `mkdtemp("/tmp/kanxeo_test_<name>_XXXXXX")` convention `test_pki.c`/`test_installer.c`/`test_boot_ab.c` already each hand-rolled independently), and to derive every direct-filesystem path they themselves touch (fixture image roots, state-file reads for persistence checks, `reset_state()`'s own cleanup targets) from that same directory rather than a hardcoded `/var/lib/kanxeo/...` literal -- the same class of hidden coupling that caused the original incident, just one level removed.

**A real, pre-existing test bug this surfaced, not introduced**: `test_images.c`'s "`base` is protected from deletion" check never actually created a `base` image itself -- it silently depended on one already existing from whatever prior state happened to be on disk, true by accident under the old shared-path setup, never true under a genuinely fresh isolated directory (`image_delete()` checks the rootfs exists on disk before checking the protected-name rule, so a never-created `base` 404s instead of 400ing). Fixed by having the test create `base` via the real API first.

Verified: full clean rebuild (`make clean && make`), zero warnings; all 16 daemon-linked tests plus all 7 runtime-library tests pass under isolation; the live daemon's own real state (5 containers, `lan1`/`wan1` networks) confirmed byte-for-byte unaffected by the full test run via direct `curl` before and after.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** concurrent test runs still can't share one `--data-dir=` (not attempted -- each invocation gets its own fresh directory per run, sequentially, matching how this suite has always been run); this closes the specific default-path collision, not general test-suite concurrency.

## Phase 30 part 5 (done): documentation audit -- `docs/` restructured, one subdirectory per document kind

A meticulous, user-requested audit covering the whole codebase and documentation set, run immediately after part 4 above landed: git hygiene validated (nothing uncommitted/unpushed at the start of the audit), a systematic check for orphaned files (every tracked `.c`/`.h` confirmed referenced from the Makefile or `#include`d somewhere; every other tracked file -- docs, recipes, web assets, kernel config, signing certs -- confirmed to have a real, current purpose), a check for duplicate/parallel implementations (none found -- one JSON writer, one shared `test_image_fixture.c` for test scaffolding, distinct `mkdtemp()` scratch dirs per test are legitimately separate, not duplicated, purposes), a check for stop-gap markers (`TODO`/`FIXME`/`XXX`/`HACK` -- none found; the only regex hits were `mkdtemp()` `XXXXXX` templates and a documented `\uXXXX` JSON-escape boundary), and one genuine dead-code instance found and removed (`test_container_files.c`'s unused `json_str_field()`, a leftover from copying the pattern out of a sibling test file that does use it).

**The user's own explicit ask, from an earlier message in this same session**: nothing but `README.md` should live in `docs/`'s own root. `docs/MISSION.md`, `docs/ROADMAP.md`, and `docs/architecture.svg` moved to `docs/mission/`, `docs/roadmap/`, and `docs/architecture/` respectively, joining `docs/adr/` and `docs/api/` as the full set -- one subdirectory per *kind* of document (charter, phase log, decision record, API contract, diagram), never per topic or per phase. A new `docs/README.md` was written as the actual index this taxonomy needed: what lives where, which document answers which question, and the naming convention itself, without duplicating any of the content it points at.

Every cross-reference to the three moved files was found and fixed, not just the obvious markdown links: `CLAUDE.md`'s own Documentation Map table, root `README.md`, `docs/api/README.md`, `docs/adr/README.md`, this file's own self-references, all 45 ADR bodies (pure link-hygiene fixes, not decision-content edits -- confirmed against `docs/adr/0000-adr-process.md`'s own append-only rule, which governs reversing a *decision*, not repairing a broken path), and doc-comments embedded inside C source (`daemon/include/{dns,persist,registry}.h`, `daemon/src/main.c`, `image/src/{kanxeo-install,mkbootroot}.c`, `image/kernel/qemu-part1.config`, four files under `test/`) and the architecture diagram's own two embedded text labels. **A deliberate exception, reasoned through rather than defaulted into**: `CHANGELOG.md`'s own historical entries (e.g. "`docs/ROADMAP.md`: Phase 11 marked done") were left referring to the path that was genuinely correct at the time each entry was written -- `CHANGELOG.md`'s job is a chronological record, and rewriting old entries to a path that didn't exist yet would misrepresent when the restructuring actually happened, the same reasoning that keeps git commit messages themselves un-rewritten.

Root `README.md` got a real content pass alongside the path fixes, not just mechanical find-and-replace: its own Status table had been stale since Phase 16 (14 shipped phases undocumented there, though always present in `docs/roadmap/ROADMAP.md` itself) and now runs through Phase 30; its "Using the installed system" walkthrough was missing any mention of `kanxeoctl console`/the web dashboard's own Console tab, both real, shipped, user-facing capabilities since Phase 30 parts 1-3.

Verified: full clean rebuild, zero warnings; a repo-wide `git grep` for all three old paths confirms zero remaining hits outside the two deliberately-preserved exceptions above (`CHANGELOG.md`'s historical entries, `docs/mission/MISSION.md`'s own note explaining its rename).

**Deferred at the time, closed out the same session:** `docs/architecture/architecture.svg`'s own actual diagram content was left unchanged here, still stating "as of Phase 16" -- see Phase 30 part 6 immediately below, where the user asked for it directly.

## Phase 30 part 6 (done): redraw `architecture.svg` for real -- content, not just paths

The user's direct follow-up to part 5's own named boundary: redraw the diagram itself, not just fix its embedded links. Two genuine shape changes since the Phase 16 baseline earned new boxes (the diagram's own standing rule, per `CLAUDE.md`'s Documentation Map, is to reflect *shape* -- a new module, a new host-level component, a new client surface -- not every phase's internal detail):

- **A new daemon module box, "Exec / Console"** (`setns + PTY exec`, `WebSocket relay`) -- the hand-rolled RFC 6455 WebSocket + `setns()`/double-fork exec mechanism (ADR-0043) is a genuinely new capability class alongside Registry/Network/DNS-PKI/Pkg-Image/Device/System, not an extension of any existing one. A new dashed arrow, styled and colored to match this box, runs from it down to the containers layer, routed along the runtime-library box's own right margin specifically so it doesn't visually cross through the "sysctls + device nodes" box's interior -- confirmed by rendering, not assumed (below).
- **A new client-surface box, "Physical Console"** (video tty0 + serial ttyS0, PID1-spawned `kanxeoctl`, ADR-0034) -- a real, distinct way of reaching `kanxeod`'s own REST API that predates this same phase (Phases 18-19) but had never been drawn: `kanxeod` itself, running as PID 1, spawns a supervised `kanxeoctl` shell directly on the physical/serial console, talking loopback HTTP rather than a network hop, architecturally distinct from both the CLI-over-the-network and the web dashboard boxes already shown.

Every other module box's text was tightened to fit the now-7-wide (was 6) daemon module row (128px each, down from 150px) and refreshed for current capabilities without cramming in every ADR number -- a deliberate legibility trade-off: the small module row now states *what* each module does in plain words (`recipes via API`, `VLAN, gateway-opt`, `shutdown, reboot / update, backup`) and leaves ADR citations to the larger, roomier boxes (runtime library row, Persistent State column, Host OS layer) where they already live. The `pkg/recipes` box in the Persistent State column was updated from "source-based package builds" to "REST-managed catalog (ADR-0040)", reflecting Phase 26. The `kanxeoctl`/Web Dashboard client boxes gained one-line mentions of the console subcommand and the Console tab respectively. Title, subtitle, and footer all updated from "Phase 16" to "Phase 30".

**Verified by actually rendering it, not just by manual coordinate math**: no SVG renderer exists in this sandbox by default, so `librsvg2-bin` (`rsvg-convert`) was installed specifically to check this diagram, and every edited region was rendered to PNG and visually inspected at full and cropped/zoomed resolution -- confirming no text overflows its box, no unintended overlaps, and the new exec/console arrow's right-margin routing genuinely clears every box it passes (a risk manual bounding-box arithmetic alone, the prior session's own method absent a renderer, could not fully rule out). A programmatic pre-check (Python + `xml.etree.ElementTree`, per-character width estimation against each box's own width) caught nothing wrong in the edited regions before the render confirmed it directly.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** the diagram still doesn't depict the installer (`kanxeo-install`) as its own swimlane, the dual-console PTY relay it uses (Phase 28), or the backup/restore data flow (Phase 17) as a distinct arrow -- all real, but judged not to rise to "new box or arrow" territory the way Exec/Console and Physical Console did; named here rather than silently scoped out.

## Phase 31 (done): a wireless-tools recipe set -- `procps`, `iw`, `hostapd`

Raised directly by the user (`ip` was already covered by the existing `iproute2.recipe`; `ps` via `procps`, plus `iw` and `hostapd`, were the real new asks): three new recipes, verified the same two-phase way every prior recipe in this set has been -- a real build first, then a full install through the actual `kanxeod` pipeline, ending with every installed binary confirmed to actually run inside a real running container, not just present on disk.

`procps.recipe` (4.0.6, ps/top/free/kill/pgrep/pkill/pidof/pidwait/pmap/pwdx/slabtop/tload/uptime/vmstat/w/watch/hugetop, plus `sysctl`) builds from a raw GitLab release-tag archive (no pre-generated `./configure`), surfacing a real cascading toolchain-staging gap chased one missing component at a time via direct chroot reproduction against the daemon's own `pkgbuild` sandbox (the async pipeline captures no build logs, so this is the only way to see the real failure): `autopoint` (separate Debian package from `gettext` itself), then `/usr/share/gettext` and `/usr/share/aclocal` (autopoint's/gettext's own data and macro directories), then `/usr/share/automake-1.16` (automake's real Perl implementation, distinct from its `/usr/bin/automake` wrapper), then `/usr/share/libtool` (libtoolize's own build-aux templates), then `/usr/share/aclocal-1.16` (automake's own version-specific macro directory, distinct from the already-staged `/usr/share/aclocal`), and finally `/usr/share/misc` -- the real `config.guess`/`config.sub` GNU triplet scripts, which `/usr/share/automake-1.16`'s own copies turn out to be symlinks to (`ls -la` confirmed), so staging automake-1.16 alone still left `./configure` failing. All six now permanent `extras[]` entries in `test/test_image_fixture.c`, shared by both the test/daemon toolchain-staging path and `image/src/mktoolchainimage.c`'s squashfs builder (one function, One Source of Truth).

`iw.recipe` (6.17, the nl80211-based CLI for wireless device configuration) built clean on the first real pipeline attempt -- its `libnl-3`/`libnl-genl-3` link dependency was already covered by the existing wholesale toolchain copy, needing only the same real-runtime-library staging pattern (`pkg_install()` copies both the SONAME symlink and its versioned target, ipset.recipe's own libmnl precedent) every prior libnl/libssl-linking recipe already established.

`hostapd.recipe` (2.11, the IEEE 802.11 AP/authentication daemon) reads a hand-provided `hostapd/.config` (its own Kconfig-lite convention, no `./configure`), built from upstream's own `defconfig` baseline plus `CONFIG_IEEE80211W`/`CONFIG_SAE` added for WPA3-Personal. **A real recipe-authorship bug found only by comparing the daemon's own reported package manifest against the real build output, not by trusting a green "installed" state**: `pkg_build()`'s first line is `cd hostapd` (building from the subdirectory only, not the top-level source tree, since `wpa_supplicant/`'s client-side counterpart in the same release isn't built here), and because `pkg_build()`/`pkg_install()` run in the *same* shell session/cwd (`daemon/src/pkg.c`: `. recipe.sh; cd src && pkg_build && pkg_install`), that `cd` was still in effect when `pkg_install()` ran -- its original `cp hostapd/hostapd hostapd/hostapd_cli "$dir/"` therefore looked for a nonexistent doubly-nested path and failed silently (no `set -e` in this recipe contract), while the unrelated absolute-path `libnl`/`libssl` copies on the next lines still succeeded, so the install still reported "installed" with a manifest quietly missing both real binaries. Caught by chrooting into the merged image and finding `usr/sbin/hostapd` absent despite a clean install state; fixed by making the `cp` paths bare (`hostapd`/`hostapd_cli`), matching the cwd `pkg_install()` actually inherits.

Verified end-to-end against a live daemon carrying its real 5-container VRRP/OSPF demo topology throughout (restart-survival re-confirmed via `restart: "always"` before each of the two daemon restarts this phase needed): all three packages installed for real onto a `wifitools` image through the actual `kanxeod` pipeline; every binary confirmed to actually execute inside a real running container (not a bare chroot, which lacks `/proc` and produced a real-but-expected `ps` failure along the way) via `kanxeoctl console --cmd=`: `ps` printed a real process list, `iw` printed its usage/version banner (6.17), `hostapd` printed its version banner and usage (2.11) exactly as upstream does with no config file supplied. `top`/`watch`'s own ncurses `setupterm()` call was confirmed (via `strace`) to need a terminfo database this minimal from-scratch image doesn't ship -- a real, separate, named gap (no `ncurses-base`-equivalent recipe exists yet), not a defect in this phase's own recipes; the container-liveness check instead used `vmstat` (plain-stdout interval output, no curses dependency). Full clean rebuild, zero warnings; `test_pkg` (the test most directly exercising toolchain staging) re-run clean against its own isolated `--data-dir=`.

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** no `ncurses-base`/terminfo-database recipe, so `top`/`watch` remain uninstallable-as-genuinely-usable on any from-scratch image today (they build and link fine; only their curses runtime dependency is unmet) -- a real, generic gap this phase's own verification surfaced but didn't attempt to solve, since it's a base-image/runtime-data concern shared by any future curses-linked package, not something this recipe set should own; `hostapd` was verified only for process startup and CLI banners, not a real AP session (no wireless hardware/PCI passthrough exists in this environment to drive `nl80211` for real), the same honest boundary already accepted for anything in this project needing physical wireless hardware.

## Phase 32 (done): image detail view -- add/install/remove packages inline, tabbed to declutter

Direct user follow-up in the same request that asked for Phase 31's recipes: "let's make the image management more intuitive... add/delete recipes from images" and "the image detail display gets decluttered" into tab/button-separated sections instead of two always-both-visible flat tables. An "image version" concept was asked for alongside this but flagged rather than built -- `Image` (`docs/api/openapi.yaml`) has only a `name` field; images have no version of their own (only individually-installed packages inside them do, already shown per-row in the packages table), so no new field was invented for a concept that doesn't exist in the data model.

`web/index.html`'s `#view-image-detail` restructured to the same `.detail-topbar` / `.tab-bar` / `.tab-panel[data-tab]` pattern the container detail view already established (Phase 30 part 3) -- reused, not reinvented: the page's one generic tab-switching handler (`app.js`, wired once against every `.tab-bar .tab-button` on the page) picked up the new tabs with zero new JS, since it resolves panels via `tabBar.parentElement`, not a hardcoded id. Two tabs replace the old always-visible pair: "Containers using this image" (unchanged content) and "Packages installed", which now also holds the full recipe catalog inline, each recipe row offering "Install onto this image" or, once installed, "Remove from image" -- both driven straight from the recipe/package row via the same `POST /v1/pkg/install` / `DELETE /v1/pkg/{name@image}` endpoints the standalone Packages section's own modal-based forms already use (ADR-0040), just scoped to whichever image is on screen instead of requiring the name/image to be typed into a separate form. An "Add recipe..." button opens that same shared recipe-upload modal (`openModal("pkg-recipe-form", ...)`) rather than duplicating its file-upload logic -- recipes stay a genuinely global catalog (`daemon/include/pkg.h`); this phase is a UI/UX convenience layer over that catalog, not a data-model change, matching Phase 26's own architecture.

Verified data/schema/logic-level against a real running daemon (no browser in this sandbox, the same honest boundary already named for the Phase 29/30 dashboard work): confirmed the daemon serves the edited `web/index.html`/`app.js` directly with no build step, confirmed `cache.pkgList`/`cache.pkgRecipes` are unconditionally refreshed every poll cycle regardless of which view is on screen (so the new tables never render stale/empty on first load), confirmed all referenced DOM ids exist on both sides, and confirmed the underlying install/remove endpoints this view now drives inline already work end-to-end (proven directly in Phase 31's own verification, same session). `node --check app.js` clean. The user's own click-through in a real browser remains the final confirmation, same as every prior dashboard phase.

## Phase 33 (done): a real dev-toolchain recipe set -- 16 packages, a self-hosting `dev` image, and the platform's first genuinely new class of build-time dependency

Raised two ways in the same short window: the user asked directly whether an `lldap` recipe already existed (it did, but had silently fallen out of the live daemon's own recipe catalog after an earlier `--data-dir=` reset this session -- the recipe file itself was untouched, re-uploaded and reinstalled cleanly, a real Rust+WASM build, confirmed installed), then asked for a real, from-source dev toolchain -- individual recipes per tool rather than one bundled image, so pieces can be added to any image as needed. This phase built that toolchain: `m4`, `binutils`, `bison`, `flex`, `make`, `gawk`, `autoconf`, `automake`, `libtool`, `pkgconf`, `perl`, `python`, `gcc`, `libc-dev`, and `coreutils` -- fourteen new recipes plus `lldap` reloaded, all installed onto a new `dev` target image and proven, at the very end, to genuinely self-host: compile, link, and run a real C program, and separately run real Python, entirely inside a real running Kanxeo container with zero manual host intervention.

Every recipe in the small-tools tier followed the same shape already established by this project's prior recipe phases -- a real upstream tarball, checksum-verified against two independent sources where a second source existed, a plain `./configure && make` (or, for `gcc`, a real multi-source vendored build), and a `pkg_install()` staging exactly the real runtime libraries `ldd` showed were actually needed. Several of these, though, surfaced real, load-bearing *runtime* dependencies that only show up once a tool actually runs, not while it builds -- caught by inspecting the built artifacts directly (`file`, shebang lines, `strings`) rather than trusting a clean build alone: `bison` hardcodes the absolute path `/usr/bin/m4` into its own generated wrapper and shells out to it at parse-generation time, so `bison.recipe` declares `pkg_depends="m4"`; `autoconf`'s own generated tools (`autoheader`, `autom4te`, `autoreconf`, `autoscan`, `autoupdate`, `ifnames`) are all real `#!/usr/bin/perl` scripts, and its own generated `configure` hardcodes the bare command name `gawk` (found via `AC_PROG_AWK`'s own real search order -- gawk, then mawk, then nawk, then awk -- resolved at *this build's* configure time, not deferred), so `autoconf.recipe` declares `pkg_depends="m4 perl gawk"`. `gawk` itself turned out to need a real `libreadline`/`libtinfo` runtime dependency of its own (rarely-exercised interactive-mode support), staged with the same SONAME-symlink-plus-real-target pattern every other recipe's runtime libs already use.

`python.recipe`'s own first build attempt printed a real, actionable summary -- "The necessary bits to build these optional modules were not found: `_ctypes` `_sqlite3` `_bz2` `_lzma` `_dbm` `_uuid`" -- not a guess, gcc's own configure output naming the exact gap. Six real host `-dev` packages (`libffi-dev`, `libbz2-dev`, `liblzma-dev`, `libgdbm-dev`, `uuid-dev`, `libsqlite3-dev`) were installed on the build host itself, flowing into the isolated build sandbox for free via the same wholesale `/usr/{include,lib}` toolchain copy every prior recipe phase already relies on; a second, clean build then produced every one of those modules except `_dbm` (its modern replacement, `_gdbm`, already builds and covers the same need) and `_tkinter` (a GUI toolkit binding with no real use in a headless container -- the same judgment call this project's own framework-free web console already made against a GUI dependency, named here rather than silently dropped).

`gcc.recipe` is the centerpiece of this phase and the one that found the most genuinely new territory. GCC needs three build-time math libraries (`gmp`, `mpfr`, `mpc`) plus `isl` (its graphite loop-optimization dependency) that its own `contrib/download_prerequisites` script would normally fetch live -- not possible inside this project's isolated, network-less build container. All four are vendored as real, checksum-verified multi-source entries (ADR-0036) at the exact versions that script itself pins (read directly from `gcc-16.1.0/contrib/download_prerequisites`, not guessed at); `gmp`/`mpfr`/`mpc` verified byte-identical against `ftp.gnu.org` directly, `isl` (no GNU release of its own) verified against `gcc.gnu.org/pub/gcc/infrastructure/`, its own documented canonical distribution point for exactly this use. The recipe's own first real daemon install attempt failed in about twenty seconds -- an instant parse-level failure, not a real build failure -- because `pkg_source=`/`pkg_sha256=` had been written as multi-line values with literal embedded newlines inside the quotes; ADR-0036's real format is a single line, space-separated (confirmed by comparing directly against `lldap.recipe`'s own already-working multi-source recipe), and additional sources beyond index 0 land as *plain, unextracted files* at `/build/extra/<basename-of-their-own-URL>`, never pre-extracted (confirmed by reading `daemon/src/pkg.c`'s own source comments describing that exact convention) -- both fixed, and `pkg_build()` now extracts each of the four itself before configuring.

The real build itself -- single-stage (`--disable-bootstrap`, using this build host's own already-trusted gcc 12.2 to compile gcc 16.1.0, not a 3-stage self-verifying bootstrap, which exists for gcc's own release engineering and buys nothing here), C and C++ only (`--enable-languages=c,c++`; Fortran/Ada/Go/D/Objective-C/Modula-2 are real gcc frontends but out of scope for this recipe set) -- is, by real wall-clock time, comfortably the longest build in this project to date, run twice in full: once locally for initial verification, once for real through the daemon's own isolated pipeline. `cc1`/`cc1plus`/`lto1` ship completely unstripped by default, roughly 400MB each (~1.8GB for the whole `destdir`); an explicit `strip --strip-unneeded` pass in `pkg_install()` brings the real installed footprint down to roughly 300MB, confirmed not to affect gcc's own ability to invoke any of them.

Proving this gcc actually *works* -- not just that it installed -- surfaced two genuinely new findings, neither of which is a Kanxeo bug, both real GCC behavior specific to this minimal, `/proc`-light container environment. First: gcc must be invoked by its absolute path (`/usr/bin/gcc`), never a bare `gcc` resolved via `$PATH` -- confirmed directly via `gcc -v`, side by side: bare-name invocation computes a wrong *relative* `-iprefix` (`../lib/gcc/x86_64-pc-linux-gnu/16.1.0/` instead of the correct absolute path), which breaks `cc1` invocation with a genuinely misleading `posix_spawnp: No such file or directory` error that reads exactly like a missing file, but isn't one. Second: `collect2` (gcc's own linker-invocation driver, a separate binary from the `gcc` front end) searches for the real `ld` via the *same* private `COMPILER_PATH` gcc itself uses to find `cc1` -- never falling back to `$PATH`, despite `ld` genuinely being on `$PATH` the whole time (`binutils` is a real `pkg_depends=`) -- confirmed by symlinking `/usr/bin/ld` into gcc's own private prefix directory and watching `collect2: fatal error: cannot find 'ld'` disappear; fixed permanently with a real `ln -sf /usr/bin/ld "$PKG_DESTDIR/usr/libexec/gcc/x86_64-pc-linux-gnu/16.1.0/ld"` in `pkg_install()`. Both findings point at the same underlying shape: `cc1`/`cc1plus`/`lto1`/`collect2`'s own `ld` lookup are the platform's first genuinely new *class* of dependency -- every one of this project's 100-plus prior recipe packages only ever needed runtime-*linked* shared libraries (`ldd`-visible, resolved by the dynamic linker at program start), never a private subprocess/helper-binary search path a driver program shells out to at compile time.

Fixing `ld` resolution immediately surfaced the next, deeper gap: `collect2: fatal error: cannot find 'ld'` gave way to `cannot find crt1.o: No such file or directory` the moment `ld` itself could run. `crt1.o`/`crti.o`/`crtn.o` (the real C-runtime startup/teardown object files linked into *every* dynamically-linked ELF binary on this platform) and glibc's own development headers (`stdio.h` and the rest of a real, complete `/usr/include` tree) are provided by glibc's own `-dev` package on every real distro (`libc6-dev` on Debian) -- a genuinely separate concern from gcc's own `crtbegin.o`/`crtend.o`/`libgcc.a`, and something no recipe in this entire project had ever needed to stage into a *target* image before, because every prior recipe only ever produced an already-linked, finished binary. Building glibc itself from source is its own giant, multi-day undertaking with its own kernel-header and bootstrap intricacies -- squarely out of scope, the same "too complex to reasonably build here, stage the real pre-built host copy instead" call this project already made for its Go and Rust toolchains (`test/test_image_fixture.c`'s own `extras[]`, staged for the *build sandbox's* own use). `libc-dev.recipe` makes the equivalent real files available in a *target* image instead: the real, complete `/usr/include` tree (~88MB, wholesale-staged rather than hand-curated, since it inevitably also carries every other dev package already on this build host -- openssl, zlib, ncurses, sqlite3, and so on -- the same "too fragile to hand-curate perfectly" reasoning already applied to gcc's own runtime-library staging) plus `crt1.o`/`crti.o`/`crtn.o`/`Scrt1.o`/`gcrt1.o`/`Mcrt1.o`, copied directly from the toolchain sandbox's own already-present real glibc-dev, at `/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu` -- this project's own real runtime-library path, confirmed via `ls -la` that `/lib` is a genuine directory here rather than a `/usr/lib` symlink the way a real usrmerge'd host has it, and confirmed via `gcc -v`'s own `LIBRARY_PATH` output that gcc's search never actually reaches `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu` (where the build host itself keeps them) on this platform at all. `libc.so` turned out to be a real GNU ld script, not a symlink (`GROUP ( /lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libc.so.6 /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libc_nonshared.a AS_NEEDED ( /lib64/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2 ) )`, confirmed via `cat`) -- its second path hardcodes a directory that doesn't exist anywhere on this project's own image layout, rewritten via a real `sed` substitution at install time to reference `/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu` for both paths instead, matching where `libc_nonshared.a` is actually staged.

`coreutils.recipe` was raised twice, independently, within the same short window -- the user asked directly ("claude we need a recepie for coreutils right?"), landing at almost exactly the moment this same phase's own self-hosting verification hit the identical gap from the other direction: no `cat`/`ls`/`mkdir`/etc existed anywhere on the `dev` image, only bash's own builtins, so a `cat > file << EOF` heredoc in a verification script silently failed with "command not found" and left a stale or empty file behind -- which, for a while, looked like a real gcc/link bug ("undefined reference to `main`") before being traced back to the missing `cat` itself, not gcc at all. `coreutils`'s own `configure` also refused to run as root by default -- a real, deliberate safety guard against running its *own test suite* as root on a real developer's workstation (root bypasses several permission checks those tests rely on), not a guard against a legitimate isolated build; this project's build container has no non-root user at all, and this recipe never runs `make check`, so `FORCE_UNSAFE_CONFIGURE=1` is set in `pkg_build()`. The real ~100-utility set (`cat`, `ls`, `cp`, `mv`, `rm`, `mkdir`, `chmod`, `chown`, `sort`, `head`, `tail`, `cut`, `tr`, `basename`, `dirname`, `readlink`, `realpath`, `stat`, `touch`, `date`, `env`, `printf`, `sleep`, `timeout`, `yes`, `tee`, and the rest) needed only `libcap` (`ls`'s own file-capability display) and `libcrypto` (the `sha*sum` family's real OpenSSL-backed digests) beyond libc, both staged the same way as every other recipe's runtime libs.

Every one of the sixteen packages in this phase went through the same real, two-phase discipline this project already applies everywhere: a real local build and install first (verified directly with `ldd`, `nm`, and real chroot execution -- not assumed from a clean build), then a completely separate, full real install through the actual `kanxeod` pipeline (the isolated, network-less build container), confirmed via `GET /v1/pkg` showing `state: "installed"` with a real, substantial file list every time. The phase closes with a genuine, first-of-its-kind proof for this whole project: a real running Kanxeo container, built entirely from a `dev` image assembled from these sixteen recipes, that writes a real `hello.c` with its own real `cat`, compiles and links it with its own from-source `gcc` against its own from-source-staged `libc-dev`, runs the resulting binary, and separately runs real Python 3.13 code -- with zero manual host intervention, entirely through the daemon's own REST API:

```
gcc_compile_exit=0
hello self-hosted gcc
gcc_run_exit=0
```
```
Python 3.13.5
4
```

**Not designed or built yet, per Zen:** no `ncurses-base`/terminfo-database recipe still exists (named already in Phase 31, still true) -- `top`/`watch` remain uninstallable-as-genuinely-usable on the `dev` image for the same reason as everywhere else in this project. Go and Rust remain build-sandbox-only toolchain extras (`test/test_image_fixture.c`'s own `extras[]`), not installable *target-image* packages the way `gcc`/`python`/`perl` now are -- a real, consistent next step if this project's own dev-toolchain work continues, not attempted here since the ask was specifically a C/Python-capable toolchain. `pkgconf`/`libtool`/`automake`/`autoconf` are all present and real, but no actual autotools-based *project* has yet been built end-to-end inside a `dev` container using them (only the underlying compiler/linker pair was proven) -- a natural, real follow-on verification, not claimed here.

## Phase 34 (done): container lifecycle completeness -- start/pause/unpause, and two real bugs a stopped container's own disappearance was hiding (ADR-0045)

Raised directly by the user, reporting a concrete symptom: `POST .../stop` a container and it simply vanishes -- no way to bring it back short of a full daemon restart, and even that only works for `restart:"always"`/`"on-failure"`. Investigated before writing any code: `GET /v1/containers` (`registry_write_json_list()`) only ever iterated the *live* in-memory registry, and `POST .../stop` (ADR-0027, by design) removes exactly that live entry while deliberately keeping the persisted definition -- so a stopped container was never actually gone, just unreachable. Confirmed with the user: also build real cgroup-freezer pause/resume (not settle for the start fix alone).

`POST /v1/containers/{name}/start` closes the actual gap: idempotent if already live, 404 if no persisted definition exists at all, otherwise replays the definition's own stored body through `create_container_from_body()` -- the same function `containerdef_autostart_all()` and the crash-restart path already use, one source of truth for "how a definition becomes a live container." `POST .../pause` and `.../unpause` turned out to need no new kernel-facing mechanism at all: `container_handle.cgroup_fd` (`include/container.h`) has been an `O_PATH` fd on each container's own real cgroup v2 leaf since Phase 1, kept open for the container's whole life -- `openat(cgroup_fd, "cgroup.freeze", O_WRONLY)` is all pause/unpause needed. Verified against the kernel's own ground truth, not just the REST response: `/sys/fs/cgroup/<name>/cgroup.events` genuinely reports `frozen 1`/`frozen 0`, and a frozen process's own CPU time (`/proc/<pid>/stat`) measurably stops advancing while paused.

Wiring pause all the way through surfaced a real correctness bug before it ever shipped: a cgroup v2 freezer blocks signal delivery to every task inside it, so `registry_remove()` (the one shared kill path both `POST .../stop` and `DELETE` already reuse) issuing `SIGKILL` at an already-frozen container would queue the signal but never actually terminate the process -- confirmed directly, a real hang, not a hypothetical. Fixed by thawing first (`registry_set_paused(e, 0)`) immediately before the existing `SIGKILL` call.
